b   An investor is analysing two securities, Security X and Security Y, based on 10
different market scenarios. The expected returns and probabilities are as follows:
********** 4 **********
--- page 5 ---
Market Scenario Probability Return X (%) Return Y (%)
Scenario 1   0.25   16   11
Scenario 2   0.50   12   10
Scenario 3   0.25   8   9
Required:
1. Calculate the Expected Return of both securities.
2. Calculate the Standard Deviation of returns for both securities.
3. Suggest which security is more consistent and justify.   (10 Marks)
1. Expected Return (3 Marks)
Formula:
𝐸(𝑅) = ∑(𝑃× 𝑅)
Security X
= (0.25 × 16) + (0.50 × 12) + (0.25 × 8)
= 4 + 6 + 2 = 12%
Security Y
= (0.25 × 11) + (0.50 × 10) + (0.25 × 9)
= 2.75 + 5 + 2.25 = 10%
2. Standard Deviation (5 Marks)
Formula:
𝜎= √∑𝑃(𝑅−𝑅ˉ)2
Security X
Return   Deviation (R−12)   Square   P × Square
16   4   16   4
12   0   0   0
8   -4   16   4
𝑉𝑎𝑟𝑖𝑎𝑛𝑐𝑒= 8 ⇒𝜎= √8 = 2.83%
Security Y
Return   Deviation (R−10)   Square   P × Square
11   1   1   0.25
10   0   0   0
9   -1   1   0.25
𝑉𝑎𝑟𝑖𝑎𝑛𝑐𝑒= 0.5 ⇒𝜎= √0.5 = 0.71%
3. Consistency Decision (2 Marks)
•   Security X: Higher return (12%) but higher variability (2.83%)
•   Security Y: Lower return (10%) but very low variability (0.71%)
Conclusion:
•   Security Y is more consistent due to lower standard deviation.
Q4   20
a   “Options are considered superior hedging instruments compared to forwards and 10
futures.” Evaluate this statement with examples. [10 marks]
Derivative instruments are widely used for hedging risk. Among them, options are
often considered superior due to their flexibility and limited risk exposure.
However, this superiority must be evaluated critically.
Flexibility of Options
********** 5 **********
--- page 6 ---
Options provide the right but not the obligation to execute a contract.
Example:
An investor buys a put option to protect against a fall in share price.
If price falls, he exercises the option.
If price rises, he ignores the option and benefits from the market.
This ensures downside protection and upside participation.
Forwards are binding contracts with no flexibility.
Options allow the investor to choose whether to exercise.
Hence, options are more flexible than forwards
Futures involve daily settlement and unlimited loss potential.
Options limit loss to the premium paid.
Thus, options provide risk limitation.
Options require payment of premium, making them costly.
Not always suitable for all hedging needs.

Q5   20
a   Q.
A trader expects a significant movement in the price of a stock, currently trading
at ₹100, but is uncertain about the direction. The trader adopts a Long Strangle
strategy using the following options:
• Buy a Call option with Strike Price ₹105 at a premium of ₹4
• Buy a Put option with Strike Price ₹95 at a premium of ₹3
Required:
1. Explain the Long Strangle strategy and its structure.
2. Calculate the total cost and breakeven points.
3. Determine the profit or loss if the stock price at expiry is:
o ₹85
o ₹100
o ₹120
4. Comment on the suitability and risk of the strategy. (10 Marks)
Answer
1. Strategy Explanation (3 Marks)
• A Long Strangle is a non-directional options strategy used when large
price movement is expected.
•   It involves:
1. Buying an OTM Call option
2. Buying an OTM Put option
• Both options have:
1. Same expiry
2. Same underlying asset
Profit arises when price moves sharply beyond either strike price. 2.
Total Cost & Breakeven (3 Marks)
Step 1: Total Premium Paid
= 𝟒+ 𝟑= ₹𝟕
Step 2: Breakeven Points
• Upper BEP = Call Strike + Premium = 105 + 7 = ₹112
• Lower BEP = Put Strike − Premium = 95 − 7 = ₹88
3. Profit/Loss Calculation (2 Marks)
Case 1: Price = ₹85
•   Call payoff = 0
•   Put payoff = 95 − 85 = 10
• Net Profit = 10 − 7 = ₹3 (Profit)
Case 2: Price = ₹100
•   Call payoff = 0
•   Put payoff = 0
• Net Loss = ₹7 (Maximum Loss)
Case 3: Price = ₹120
•   Call payoff = 120 − 105 = 15
•   Put payoff = 0
• Net Profit = 15 − 7 = ₹8 (Profit)
********** 7 **********
--- page 8 ---
4. Suitability & Risk (2 Marks)
•   Suitable when high volatility is expected, but direction is uncertain.
• Maximum loss = Total premium (₹7)
•   Profit potential = Unlimited (upside) and substantial (downside)
b   Q.A
An option has a current Delta of 0.50 and Gamma of 0.04. The price of the
underlying asset increases by ₹5.
Required:
1. Calculate the new Delta after the price change.
2. Interpret the impact of Gamma on Delta. (5 Marks)
Q.B
Explain the concept of Theta (Θ) in options. Discuss its characteristics and
its impact on option pricing with respect to time to expiry. (5 Marks)
A. Calculation of New Delta (3 Marks)
Change in Delta=Gamma×Change in Price
𝑁𝑒𝑤 𝐷𝑒𝑙𝑡𝑎= 0.50 + 0.20 = 0.70
2. Interpretation (2 Marks)
1. Gamma shows how quickly Delta changes with price movement.
2. A higher Gamma results in larger changes in Delta, increasing sensitivity
and risk.
B. 1. Meaning of Theta (1 Mark)
Theta measures the rate of change of option price with respect
to time (time decay).
2. Nature of Theta (1 Mark)
Theta is generally negative for option buyers and positive for
option sellers.
3. Time Decay Effect (1 Mark)
Option value declines as time passes, even if price remains
unchanged.
4. Behaviour Near Expiry (1 Mark)
Time decay becomes very rapid as expiry approaches, especially
for ATM options.
5. Practical Implication (1 Mark)
Buyers lose value due to time decay, while sellers benefit from it.
Q6
a   Q.
A financial analyst is examining the relationship between spot and forward
exchange rates using the Interest Rate Parity (IRP) theory. The following data is
available:
•   Spot Exchange Rate (₹/$) = ₹83 per USD
•   Interest Rate in India = 6% p.a.
********** 8 **********
--- page 9 ---
•   Interest Rate in USA = 3% p.a.
• Time Period = 1 year
Required:
1. State the Interest Rate Parity (IRP) theory and its formula.
2. Calculate the theoretical forward exchange rate.
3.   If the actual forward rate is ₹85 per USD, identify whether an arbitrage
opportunity exists.
4. Suggest the arbitrage strategy to exploit the opportunity. (10 Marks)
Answer
1. IRP Theory & Formula (2 Marks)
•   Interest Rate Parity states that the difference in interest rates between
two countries is reflected in the forward exchange rate.
(1+𝑖𝑑𝑜𝑚𝑒𝑠𝑡𝑖𝑐)
• 𝐹= 𝑆×
(1+𝑖𝑓𝑜𝑟𝑒𝑖𝑔𝑛)
Calculation of Forward Rate (4 Marks)
1.06
𝐹= 83 ×
1.03
= 83 × 1.0291 = ₹85.41
Theoretical Forward Rate ≈ ₹85.41 per USD
Arbitrage Identification (2 Marks)
•   Theoretical Rate = ₹85.41
• Market Forward Rate = ₹85
Observation:
• Forward rate is underpriced
Arbitrage Opportunity Exists
Arbitrage Strategy (2 Marks)
Steps:
1. Borrow USD (lower interest rate = 3%)
2. Convert USD to INR at spot rate (₹83)
3. Invest in India at 6%
4. Enter forward contract to buy USD at ₹85
5. At maturity:
o Investment grows at higher rate
o Repay USD loan at lower rate
o   Profit arises from rate difference
b   Q.
A European Call and Put option on a non-dividend paying stock have the following
details:
•   Current Stock Price (S) = ₹120
•   Strike Price (K) = ₹110
• Time to Expiry (T) = 1 year
•   Risk-free Interest Rate (r) = 10% p.a.
• Market Price of Call Option (C) = ₹15
• Market Price of Put Option (P) = ₹5
Required:
1. State the Put–Call Parity relationship.
2. Verify whether the given option prices satisfy the parity condition.
3.   If not, identify the arbitrage opportunity.
4. Suggest the arbitrage strategy with steps. (10 Marks)
********** 9 **********
--- page 10 ---
Answer
1. Put–Call Parity Formula (2 Marks)
𝐶+ 𝐾𝑒−𝑟𝑇= 𝑃+ 𝑆
2. Verification of Parity (4 Marks)
Step 1: Calculate Present Value of Strike Price
𝐾𝑒−𝑟𝑇= 110 × 𝑒−0.10
𝑒−0.10 ≈0.9048
= 110 × 0.9048 = ₹99.53
Step 2: Compute LHS and RHS
• LHS = C + PV(K) = 15 + 99.53 = ₹114.53
• RHS = P + S = 5 + 120 = ₹125
3. Arbitrage Identification (2 Marks)
• LHS < RHS → ₹114.53 < ₹125
Arbitrage Opportunity Exists
4. Arbitrage Strategy (2 Marks)
Steps:
1. Buy Call + Invest PV(K)
2. Sell Put + Sell Stock
At maturity:
•   Positions offset, and profit arises due to price mismatch
Q7
a   Q.
An Indian importer enters into a forward contract with a bank to purchase USD
1,00,000 at a forward rate of ₹84 per USD for delivery after 3 months.
However, due to urgent payment requirements, the importer requests early
delivery after 2 months. At that time, the following rates prevail:
•   Spot Rate = ₹83.50 per USD
• 1-month forward premium = ₹0.20
Required:
1. Explain the concept of an early delivery (forward cancellation and
rebooking) in forex.
2. Calculate the rate at which early delivery will be executed.
3. Determine the gain or loss to the importer due to early delivery.
4. Briefly explain why banks charge or adjust rates for early delivery. (10
Marks)
Answer
1. Concept of Early Delivery (2 Marks)
•   Early delivery occurs when a forward contract is settled before its original
maturity.
•   It involves:
1. Cancellation of existing forward contract
2. Rebooking at current applicable rates
2. Calculation of Early Delivery Rate (4 Marks)
Step 1: Adjust Spot Rate for Forward Premium
Since 1 month is remaining:
𝐴𝑑𝑗𝑢𝑠𝑡𝑒𝑑 𝑅𝑎𝑡𝑒= 𝑆𝑝𝑜𝑡+ 𝑃𝑟𝑒𝑚𝑖𝑢𝑚= 83.50 + 0.20 = ₹83.70
********** 10 **********
--- page 11 ---
Early Delivery Rate = ₹83.70 per USD
3. Gain/Loss Calculation (2 Marks)
•   Original Contract Rate = ₹84
•   Early Delivery Rate = ₹83.70
𝑇𝑜𝑡𝑎𝑙 𝐺𝑎𝑖𝑛= 0.30 × 1,00,000 = ₹30,000
Importer gains ₹30,000
4. Reason for Adjustment (2 Marks)
• Banks adjust rates due to:
1. Interest rate differentials (cost of carry)
2. Market movement in spot and forward rates
3. Compensation for breaking original contract terms

Subject: Financial Derivatives

Max. Marks: 100
FINAL EXAMINATION (Acad. Year:2025-2026)
Instructions:
1. This question paper contains 12 pages.
2. Answer to each new question to be started on a fresh page.
3. Figure in right hand side indicates full marks

5. Out of remaining questions, attempt any 4 questions.

Q1   Answer briefly.   Marks
CO-1, SO-1, a   “Derivatives are instruments of risk transfer, not risk creation.” Discuss with 05
BL-   examples.
Understand
Answer: (Reward 1 mark for every correct point)
•   Derivatives transfer risk from risk-averse to risk-taking participants.
• Hedgers reduce exposure (e.g., farmers using futures).
•   Speculators assume risk in expectation of profit.
• However, excessive leverage and misuse can amplify systemic risk.
• Example: 2008 crisis showed misuse, not inherent fault of derivatives.
•   Conclusion: Derivatives primarily transfer risk, but poor usage can create
instability.
CO-1, SO-1, b   A bank suffers a major financial loss due to a cyberattack that disrupted its 05
BL-   payment systems for two days. Identify the type of risk and suggest control
Understand   measures.
& Apply
Answer:
Risk Identified: System/Technology Risk (Operational Risk) (1 mark)
Issues: (2 marks)
Weak cybersecurity infrastructure
Lack of contingency planning
Controls: (2 marks)
Strong IT security systems and firewalls
Regular audits and penetration testing
Disaster recovery and backup systems
CO-1, SO-1,   c   Explain the concept of a Futures Contract. Discuss its key features and how it
BL-Analyse   differs from a forward contract. (5 Marks)
********** 1 **********
--- page 2 ---
Answer
1. Meaning of Futures (1 Mark)
• A futures contract is a standardized agreement traded on an exchange to
buy or sell an asset at a predetermined price on a future date.
2. Key Features (2 Marks)
1. Standardization – Contract size, expiry, and terms are fixed by the
exchange.
2. Exchange Traded – Traded on organized exchanges, ensuring
transparency.
3. Margin System – Initial and maintenance margins are required.
4. Mark-to-Market (MTM) – Daily settlement of gains and losses.
3. Futures vs Forward (2 Marks)
1. Trading Platform
o Futures: Exchange traded
o Forward: Over-the-counter (OTC)
2. Risk
o Futures: Lower counterparty risk (clearing house guarantee)
o Forward: Higher counterparty risk
3. Flexibility
o Futures: Standardized
o Forward: Customized
CO-1, SO-1, d   A put option has a strike price of ₹150. The spot price is ₹140.   05
BL-Analyse   Explain the Put option from buyers and sellers prospective.
Determine moneyness and intrinsic value.
Answer:
A put option gives the buyer (holder) the right, but not the obligation, to sell an
underlying asset at a predetermined strike price on or before expiry. (1 mark)
Buyer of Put Option (Holder) (1.5 marks)
Position: Pays premium and buys the right to sell
View: Bearish (expects price to fall)
Payoff:
Profit when Spot Price < Strike Price
Loss limited to premium paid
Seller of Put Option (Writer) (1.5 marks)
Position: Receives premium and has obligation to buy
View: Bullish/Neutral (expects price to stay same or rise)
Payoff:
Profit limited to premium received
Loss occurs when Spot Price < Strike Price
Since Spot (140) < Strike (150) = ITM Put Option (1 mark)
Intrinsic Value = Strike – Spot = 150 – 140 = ₹10
The put option is profitable with value ₹10
Q2   20
a   An Indian multinational corporation has significant exposure in British Pounds
due to its operations in the United Kingdom. The current spot exchange rate is
GBP/INR 115.00. Economic projections indicate that inflation in India will be
around 7%, whereas inflation in the United Kingdom will be approximately 3%.
********** 2 **********
--- page 3 ---
The company’s finance team wants to understand the theoretical foundation of
forward rate determination and apply it to estimate future exchange rates for
both long-term and short-term horizons.
a) Derive the Fisher relationship used in determining forward exchange rates. (4
marks)
b)   Calculate   the   one-year   forward   exchange   rate.   (3   marks)
c) Calculate the forward exchange rate for a 60-day period. (3 marks)
Answer:
b   A fund manager holds a diversified equity portfolio worth ₹50,00,000. The
portfolio has a Beta (β) of 1.2. The current value of the stock index is 20,000, and
each index futures contract is valued at ₹2,00,000.
The fund manager expects market volatility and wants to hedge the portfolio
using index futures.
Required:
1. Calculate the number of futures contracts required to hedge the
portfolio.
2.   If the market falls by 10%, compute:
o Loss on portfolio
o Gain on futures position
3. Comment on the effectiveness of the hedge.   (10 Marks)
Answer
1. Number of Futures Contracts (4 Marks)
Formula: N= β×Value of Portfolio/Value of One Futures Contract
1.2 × 50,00,000   60,00,000
𝑁=   =   = 30 𝑐𝑜𝑛𝑡𝑟𝑎𝑐𝑡𝑠
2,00,000   2,00,000
Sell 30 futures contracts (since hedging against fall)
2. Gain/Loss Calculation (3 Marks)
(a) Loss on Portfolio
Market falls by 10%:
𝐿𝑜𝑠𝑠= 50,00,000 × 10% × 1.2 = ₹6,00,000
(b) Gain on Futures
𝐺𝑎𝑖𝑛= 30 × (10% × 2,00,000) = 30 × 20,000 = ₹6,00,000
3. Effectiveness of Hedge (3 Marks)
•   Loss on portfolio = ₹6,00,000
•   Gain on futures = ₹6,00,000
Net Effect = Zero (Perfect Hedge
********** 3 **********
--- page 4 ---
Q3   20
a   A. An investor believes that the price of Reliance Industries Ltd. is unlikely to rise 10
significantly in the near future. Based on this expectation, the investor
decides to earn income by writing (selling) a call option. The strike price of
the call option is ₹2,400, and the premium received is ₹30 per share. One lot
consists of 100 shares, and the investor writes one lot of call options.
At the time of maturity, the market price of the underlying stock may be
₹2350, ₹2400, ₹2450, ₹2500, or ₹2550.
You are required to calculate the net payoff for the call option writer at each
of these possible prices and comment on the risk-return profile of the
strategy.   [ 5 marks]
B. A derivatives trader expects that the price of Larsen & Toubro (L&T) shares
will not fall significantly in the short term. The trader writes put options with
a strike price of ₹3,000 and receives a premium of ₹60 per share. Each lot
consists of 25 shares, and the trader writes 4 lots.
At maturity, the stock price may be ₹3100, ₹3000, ₹2950, ₹2900, and ₹2800.
Calculate the net payoff for the trader at each price. [ 5 marks]
Answers:
A. Premium received = 30 × 100 = ₹3000
Call Writer Payoff = Premium – Max(0, Spot – Strike)
Spot Price   Net Payoff
2350   3000
2400   3000
2450 3000 – 5000 = -2000
2500   -7000
2550   -12000
Remarks: Limited profit (premium), unlimited loss.
B.
Premium = 60 × 25 × 4 = ₹6000
Net
Spot   Loss
Payoff
3100   0   6000
3000   0   6000
2950 50×100=5000   1000
2900 100×100=10000   -4000
-
2800 200×100=20000
14000
b   An investor is analysing two securities, Security X and Security Y, based on 10
different market scenarios. The expected returns and probabilities are as follows:
********** 4 **********
--- page 5 ---
Market Scenario Probability Return X (%) Return Y (%)
Scenario 1   0.25   16   11
Scenario 2   0.50   12   10
Scenario 3   0.25   8   9
Required:
1. Calculate the Expected Return of both securities.
2. Calculate the Standard Deviation of returns for both securities.
3. Suggest which security is more consistent and justify.   (10 Marks)
1. Expected Return (3 Marks)
Formula:
𝐸(𝑅) = ∑(𝑃× 𝑅)
Security X
= (0.25 × 16) + (0.50 × 12) + (0.25 × 8)
= 4 + 6 + 2 = 12%
Security Y
= (0.25 × 11) + (0.50 × 10) + (0.25 × 9)
= 2.75 + 5 + 2.25 = 10%
2. Standard Deviation (5 Marks)
Formula:
𝜎= √∑𝑃(𝑅−𝑅ˉ)2
Security X
Return   Deviation (R−12)   Square   P × Square
16   4   16   4
12   0   0   0
8   -4   16   4
𝑉𝑎𝑟𝑖𝑎𝑛𝑐𝑒= 8 ⇒𝜎= √8 = 2.83%
Security Y
Return   Deviation (R−10)   Square   P × Square
11   1   1   0.25
10   0   0   0
9   -1   1   0.25
𝑉𝑎𝑟𝑖𝑎𝑛𝑐𝑒= 0.5 ⇒𝜎= √0.5 = 0.71%
3. Consistency Decision (2 Marks)
•   Security X: Higher return (12%) but higher variability (2.83%)
•   Security Y: Lower return (10%) but very low variability (0.71%)
Conclusion:
•   Security Y is more consistent due to lower standard deviation.
Q4   20
a   “Options are considered superior hedging instruments compared to forwards and 10
futures.” Evaluate this statement with examples. [10 marks]
Derivative instruments are widely used for hedging risk. Among them, options are
often considered superior due to their flexibility and limited risk exposure.
However, this superiority must be evaluated critically.
Flexibility of Options
********** 5 **********
--- page 6 ---
Options provide the right but not the obligation to execute a contract.
Example:
An investor buys a put option to protect against a fall in share price.
If price falls, he exercises the option.
If price rises, he ignores the option and benefits from the market.
This ensures downside protection and upside participation.
Forwards are binding contracts with no flexibility.
Options allow the investor to choose whether to exercise.
Hence, options are more flexible than forwards
Futures involve daily settlement and unlimited loss potential.
Options limit loss to the premium paid.
Thus, options provide risk limitation.
Options require payment of premium, making them costly.

Q5   20
a   Q.
A trader expects a significant movement in the price of a stock, currently trading
at ₹100, but is uncertain about the direction. The trader adopts a Long Strangle
strategy using the following options:
• Buy a Call option with Strike Price ₹105 at a premium of ₹4
• Buy a Put option with Strike Price ₹95 at a premium of ₹3
Required:
1. Explain the Long Strangle strategy and its structure.
2. Calculate the total cost and breakeven points.
3. Determine the profit or loss if the stock price at expiry is:
o ₹85
o ₹100
o ₹120
4. Comment on the suitability and risk of the strategy. (10 Marks)
Answer
1. Strategy Explanation (3 Marks)
• A Long Strangle is a non-directional options strategy used when large
price movement is expected.
•   It involves:
1. Buying an OTM Call option
2. Buying an OTM Put option
• Both options have:
1. Same expiry
2. Same underlying asset
Profit arises when price moves sharply beyond either strike price. 2.
Total Cost & Breakeven (3 Marks)
Step 1: Total Premium Paid
= 𝟒+ 𝟑= ₹𝟕
Step 2: Breakeven Points
• Upper BEP = Call Strike + Premium = 105 + 7 = ₹112
• Lower BEP = Put Strike − Premium = 95 − 7 = ₹88
3. Profit/Loss Calculation (2 Marks)
Case 1: Price = ₹85
•   Call payoff = 0
•   Put payoff = 95 − 85 = 10
• Net Profit = 10 − 7 = ₹3 (Profit)
Case 2: Price = ₹100
•   Call payoff = 0
•   Put payoff = 0
• Net Loss = ₹7 (Maximum Loss)
Case 3: Price = ₹120
•   Call payoff = 120 − 105 = 15
•   Put payoff = 0
• Net Profit = 15 − 7 = ₹8 (Profit)
********** 7 **********
--- page 8 ---
4. Suitability & Risk (2 Marks)
•   Suitable when high volatility is expected, but direction is uncertain.
• Maximum loss = Total premium (₹7)
•   Profit potential = Unlimited (upside) and substantial (downside)
b   Q.A
An option has a current Delta of 0.50 and Gamma of 0.04. The price of the
underlying asset increases by ₹5.
Required:
1. Calculate the new Delta after the price change.
2. Interpret the impact of Gamma on Delta. (5 Marks)
Q.B
Explain the concept of Theta (Θ) in options. Discuss its characteristics and
its impact on option pricing with respect to time to expiry. (5 Marks)
A. Calculation of New Delta (3 Marks)
Change in Delta=Gamma×Change in Price
𝑁𝑒𝑤 𝐷𝑒𝑙𝑡𝑎= 0.50 + 0.20 = 0.70
2. Interpretation (2 Marks)
1. Gamma shows how quickly Delta changes with price movement.
2. A higher Gamma results in larger changes in Delta, increasing sensitivity
and risk.
B. 1. Meaning of Theta (1 Mark)
Theta measures the rate of change of option price with respect
to time (time decay).
2. Nature of Theta (1 Mark)
Theta is generally negative for option buyers and positive for
option sellers.
3. Time Decay Effect (1 Mark)
Option value declines as time passes, even if price remains
unchanged.
4. Behaviour Near Expiry (1 Mark)
Time decay becomes very rapid as expiry approaches, especially
for ATM options.
5. Practical Implication (1 Mark)
Buyers lose value due to time decay, while sellers benefit from it.

a   Q.
A financial analyst is examining the relationship between spot and forward
exchange rates using the Interest Rate Parity (IRP) theory. The following data is

•   Interest Rate in India = 6% p.a.

opportunity exists.
4. Suggest the arbitrage strategy to exploit the opportunity. (10 Marks)
Answer
1. IRP Theory & Formula (2 Marks)
•   Interest Rate Parity states that the difference in interest rates between
two countries is reflected in the forward exchange rate.
(1+𝑖𝑑𝑜𝑚𝑒𝑠𝑡𝑖𝑐)
• 𝐹= 𝑆×
(1+𝑖𝑓𝑜𝑟𝑒𝑖𝑔𝑛)
Calculation of Forward Rate (4 Marks)
1.06
𝐹= 83 ×
1.03
= 83 × 1.0291 = ₹85.41
Theoretical Forward Rate ≈ ₹85.41 per USD
Arbitrage Identification (2 Marks)
•   Theoretical Rate = ₹85.41
• Market Forward Rate = ₹85
Observation:
• Forward rate is underpriced
Arbitrage Opportunity Exists
Arbitrage Strategy (2 Marks)
Steps:

2. Convert USD to INR at spot rate (₹83)

b   Q.
A European Call and Put option on a non-dividend paying stock have the following
details:
•   Current Stock Price (S) = ₹120
•   Strike Price (K) = ₹110
• Time to Expiry (T) = 1 year
•   Risk-free Interest Rate (r) = 10% p.a.
• Market Price of Call Option (C) = ₹15
• Market Price of Put Option (P) = ₹5
Required:
1. State the Put–Call Parity relationship.
2. Verify whether the given option prices satisfy the parity condition.
3.   If not, identify the arbitrage opportunity.
4. Suggest the arbitrage strategy with steps. (10 Marks)
********** 9 **********
--- page 10 ---
Answer
1. Put–Call Parity Formula (2 Marks)
𝐶+ 𝐾𝑒−𝑟𝑇= 𝑃+ 𝑆
2. Verification of Parity (4 Marks)
Step 1: Calculate Present Value of Strike Price
𝐾𝑒−𝑟𝑇= 110 × 𝑒−0.10
𝑒−0.10 ≈0.9048
= 110 × 0.9048 = ₹99.53
Step 2: Compute LHS and RHS
• LHS = C + PV(K) = 15 + 99.53 = ₹114.53
• RHS = P + S = 5 + 120 = ₹125
3. Arbitrage Identification (2 Marks)
• LHS < RHS → ₹114.53 < ₹125
Arbitrage Opportunity Exists
4. Arbitrage Strategy (2 Marks)
Steps:
1. Buy Call + Invest PV(K)
2. Sell Put + Sell Stock
At maturity:
•   Positions offset, and profit arises due to price mismatch

Since 1 month is remaining:

Importer gains ₹30,000

Q1   Answer briefly.   Marks
CO-1, SO-1, a   “Derivatives are instruments of risk transfer, not risk creation.” Discuss with 05
BL-   examples.
Understand
Answer: (Reward 1 mark for every correct point)
•   Derivatives transfer risk from risk-averse to risk-taking participants.
• Hedgers reduce exposure (e.g., farmers using futures).
•   Speculators assume risk in expectation of profit.
• However, excessive leverage and misuse can amplify systemic risk.
• Example: 2008 crisis showed misuse, not inherent fault of derivatives.
•   Conclusion: Derivatives primarily transfer risk, but poor usage can create
instability.

CO-1, SO-1,   c   Explain the concept of a Futures Contract. Discuss its key features and how it
BL-Analyse   differs from a forward contract. (5 Marks)
********** 1 **********
--- page 2 ---
Answer
1. Meaning of Futures (1 Mark)
• A futures contract is a standardized agreement traded on an exchange to
buy or sell an asset at a predetermined price on a future date.
2. Key Features (2 Marks)
1. Standardization – Contract size, expiry, and terms are fixed by the
exchange.
2. Exchange Traded – Traded on organized exchanges, ensuring
transparency.
3. Margin System – Initial and maintenance margins are required.
4. Mark-to-Market (MTM) – Daily settlement of gains and losses.
3. Futures vs Forward (2 Marks)
1. Trading Platform
o Futures: Exchange traded
o Forward: Over-the-counter (OTC)
2. Risk
o Futures: Lower counterparty risk (clearing house guarantee)
o Forward: Higher counterparty risk
3. Flexibility
o Futures: Standardized
o Forward: Customized
CO-1, SO-1, d   A put option has a strike price of ₹150. The spot price is ₹140.   05
BL-Analyse   Explain the Put option from buyers and sellers prospective.
Determine moneyness and intrinsic value.
Answer:
A put option gives the buyer (holder) the right, but not the obligation, to sell an
underlying asset at a predetermined strike price on or before expiry. (1 mark)
Buyer of Put Option (Holder) (1.5 marks)
Position: Pays premium and buys the right to sell
View: Bearish (expects price to fall)
Payoff:
Profit when Spot Price < Strike Price
Loss limited to premium paid
Seller of Put Option (Writer) (1.5 marks)
Position: Receives premium and has obligation to buy
View: Bullish/Neutral (expects price to stay same or rise)
Payoff:
Profit limited to premium received
Loss occurs when Spot Price < Strike Price
Since Spot (140) < Strike (150) = ITM Put Option (1 mark)
Intrinsic Value = Strike – Spot = 150 – 140 = ₹10
The put option is profitable with value ₹10

Q4   20
a   “Options are considered superior hedging instruments compared to forwards and 10
futures.” Evaluate this statement with examples. [10 marks]
Derivative instruments are widely used for hedging risk. Among them, options are
often considered superior due to their flexibility and limited risk exposure.
However, this superiority must be evaluated critically.
Flexibility of Options
********** 5 **********
--- page 6 ---
Options provide the right but not the obligation to execute a contract.
Example:
An investor buys a put option to protect against a fall in share price.
If price falls, he exercises the option.
If price rises, he ignores the option and benefits from the market.
This ensures downside protection and upside participation.
Forwards are binding contracts with no flexibility.
Options allow the investor to choose whether to exercise.
Hence, options are more flexible than forwards
Futures involve daily settlement and unlimited loss potential.
Options limit loss to the premium paid.
Thus, options provide risk limitation.
Options require payment of premium, making them costly.
Not always suitable for all hedging needs.

b   Q.A
An option has a current Delta of 0.50 and Gamma of 0.04. The price of the
underlying asset increases by ₹5.
Required:
1. Calculate the new Delta after the price change.
2. Interpret the impact of Gamma on Delta. (5 Marks)
Q.B
Explain the concept of Theta (Θ) in options. Discuss its characteristics and
its impact on option pricing with respect to time to expiry. (5 Marks)
A. Calculation of New Delta (3 Marks)
Change in Delta=Gamma×Change in Price
𝑁𝑒𝑤 𝐷𝑒𝑙𝑡𝑎= 0.50 + 0.20 = 0.70
2. Interpretation (2 Marks)
1. Gamma shows how quickly Delta changes with price movement.
2. A higher Gamma results in larger changes in Delta, increasing sensitivity
and risk.
B. 1. Meaning of Theta (1 Mark)
Theta measures the rate of change of option price with respect
to time (time decay).
2. Nature of Theta (1 Mark)
Theta is generally negative for option buyers and positive for
option sellers.
3. Time Decay Effect (1 Mark)
Option value declines as time passes, even if price remains
unchanged.
4. Behaviour Near Expiry (1 Mark)
Time decay becomes very rapid as expiry approaches, especially
for ATM options.
5. Practical Implication (1 Mark)
Buyers lose value due to time decay, while sellers benefit from it.

a   Q.
A financial analyst is examining the relationship between spot and forward
exchange rates using the Interest Rate Parity (IRP) theory. The following data is

opportunity exists.
4. Suggest the arbitrage strategy to exploit the opportunity. (10 Marks)
Answer
1. IRP Theory & Formula (2 Marks)
•   Interest Rate Parity states that the difference in interest rates between
two countries is reflected in the forward exchange rate.
(1+𝑖𝑑𝑜𝑚𝑒𝑠𝑡𝑖𝑐)

(1+𝑖𝑓𝑜𝑟𝑒𝑖𝑔𝑛)
Calculation of Forward Rate (4 Marks)
1.06
𝐹= 83 ×
1.03
= 83 × 1.0291 = ₹85.41
Theoretical Forward Rate ≈ ₹85.41 per USD
Arbitrage Identification (2 Marks)
•   Theoretical Rate = ₹85.41
• Market Forward Rate = ₹85
Observation:
• Forward rate is underpriced
Arbitrage Opportunity Exists
Arbitrage Strategy (2 Marks)
Steps:

b   Q.
A European Call and Put option on a non-dividend paying stock have the following
details:
•   Current Stock Price (S) = ₹120
•   Strike Price (K) = ₹110
• Time to Expiry (T) = 1 year
•   Risk-free Interest Rate (r) = 10% p.a.
• Market Price of Call Option (C) = ₹15
• Market Price of Put Option (P) = ₹5
Required:
1. State the Put–Call Parity relationship.
2. Verify whether the given option prices satisfy the parity condition.
3.   If not, identify the arbitrage opportunity.
4. Suggest the arbitrage strategy with steps. (10 Marks)
********** 9 **********
--- page 10 ---
Answer
1. Put–Call Parity Formula (2 Marks)
𝐶+ 𝐾𝑒−𝑟𝑇= 𝑃+ 𝑆
2. Verification of Parity (4 Marks)
Step 1: Calculate Present Value of Strike Price
𝐾𝑒−𝑟𝑇= 110 × 𝑒−0.10
𝑒−0.10 ≈0.9048
= 110 × 0.9048 = ₹99.53
Step 2: Compute LHS and RHS
• LHS = C + PV(K) = 15 + 99.53 = ₹114.53
• RHS = P + S = 5 + 120 = ₹125
3. Arbitrage Identification (2 Marks)
• LHS < RHS → ₹114.53 < ₹125
Arbitrage Opportunity Exists
4. Arbitrage Strategy (2 Marks)
Steps:
1. Buy Call + Invest PV(K)
2. Sell Put + Sell Stock
At maturity:
•   Positions offset, and profit arises due to price mismatch

Q1   Answer briefly.   Marks
CO-1, SO-1, a   “Derivatives are instruments of risk transfer, not risk creation.” Discuss with 05
BL-   examples.
Understand
Answer: (Reward 1 mark for every correct point)
•   Derivatives transfer risk from risk-averse to risk-taking participants.
• Hedgers reduce exposure (e.g., farmers using futures).
•   Speculators assume risk in expectation of profit.
• However, excessive leverage and misuse can amplify systemic risk.
• Example: 2008 crisis showed misuse, not inherent fault of derivatives.
•   Conclusion: Derivatives primarily transfer risk, but poor usage can create
instability.

CO-1, SO-1,   c   Explain the concept of a Futures Contract. Discuss its key features and how it
BL-Analyse   differs from a forward contract. (5 Marks)
********** 1 **********
--- page 2 ---
Answer
1. Meaning of Futures (1 Mark)
• A futures contract is a standardized agreement traded on an exchange to
buy or sell an asset at a predetermined price on a future date.
2. Key Features (2 Marks)
1. Standardization – Contract size, expiry, and terms are fixed by the
exchange.
2. Exchange Traded – Traded on organized exchanges, ensuring
transparency.
3. Margin System – Initial and maintenance margins are required.
4. Mark-to-Market (MTM) – Daily settlement of gains and losses.
3. Futures vs Forward (2 Marks)
1. Trading Platform
o Futures: Exchange traded
o Forward: Over-the-counter (OTC)
2. Risk
o Futures: Lower counterparty risk (clearing house guarantee)
o Forward: Higher counterparty risk
3. Flexibility
o Futures: Standardized
o Forward: Customized
CO-1, SO-1, d   A put option has a strike price of ₹150. The spot price is ₹140.   05
BL-Analyse   Explain the Put option from buyers and sellers prospective.
Determine moneyness and intrinsic value.
Answer:
A put option gives the buyer (holder) the right, but not the obligation, to sell an
underlying asset at a predetermined strike price on or before expiry. (1 mark)
Buyer of Put Option (Holder) (1.5 marks)
Position: Pays premium and buys the right to sell
View: Bearish (expects price to fall)
Payoff:
Profit when Spot Price < Strike Price
Loss limited to premium paid
Seller of Put Option (Writer) (1.5 marks)
Position: Receives premium and has obligation to buy
View: Bullish/Neutral (expects price to stay same or rise)
Payoff:
Profit limited to premium received
Loss occurs when Spot Price < Strike Price
Since Spot (140) < Strike (150) = ITM Put Option (1 mark)
Intrinsic Value = Strike – Spot = 150 – 140 = ₹10
The put option is profitable with value ₹10

Q4   20
a   “Options are considered superior hedging instruments compared to forwards and 10
futures.” Evaluate this statement with examples. [10 marks]
Derivative instruments are widely used for hedging risk. Among them, options are
often considered superior due to their flexibility and limited risk exposure.
However, this superiority must be evaluated critically.
Flexibility of Options
********** 5 **********
--- page 6 ---
Options provide the right but not the obligation to execute a contract.
Example:
An investor buys a put option to protect against a fall in share price.
If price falls, he exercises the option.
If price rises, he ignores the option and benefits from the market.
This ensures downside protection and upside participation.
Forwards are binding contracts with no flexibility.
Options allow the investor to choose whether to exercise.
Hence, options are more flexible than forwards
Futures involve daily settlement and unlimited loss potential.
Options limit loss to the premium paid.
Thus, options provide risk limitation.
Options require payment of premium, making them costly.
Not always suitable for all hedging needs.

Q5   20
a   Q.
A trader expects a significant movement in the price of a stock, currently trading
at ₹100, but is uncertain about the direction. The trader adopts a Long Strangle
strategy using the following options:
• Buy a Call option with Strike Price ₹105 at a premium of ₹4
• Buy a Put option with Strike Price ₹95 at a premium of ₹3
Required:
1. Explain the Long Strangle strategy and its structure.
2. Calculate the total cost and breakeven points.
3. Determine the profit or loss if the stock price at expiry is:
o ₹85
o ₹100
o ₹120
4. Comment on the suitability and risk of the strategy. (10 Marks)
Answer
1. Strategy Explanation (3 Marks)
• A Long Strangle is a non-directional options strategy used when large
price movement is expected.
•   It involves:
1. Buying an OTM Call option
2. Buying an OTM Put option
• Both options have:
1. Same expiry
2. Same underlying asset
Profit arises when price moves sharply beyond either strike price. 2.
Total Cost & Breakeven (3 Marks)
Step 1: Total Premium Paid
= 𝟒+ 𝟑= ₹𝟕
Step 2: Breakeven Points
• Upper BEP = Call Strike + Premium = 105 + 7 = ₹112
• Lower BEP = Put Strike − Premium = 95 − 7 = ₹88
3. Profit/Loss Calculation (2 Marks)
Case 1: Price = ₹85
•   Call payoff = 0
•   Put payoff = 95 − 85 = 10
• Net Profit = 10 − 7 = ₹3 (Profit)
Case 2: Price = ₹100
•   Call payoff = 0
•   Put payoff = 0
• Net Loss = ₹7 (Maximum Loss)
Case 3: Price = ₹120
•   Call payoff = 120 − 105 = 15
•   Put payoff = 0
• Net Profit = 15 − 7 = ₹8 (Profit)
********** 7 **********

•   Suitable when high volatility is expected, but direction is uncertain.
• Maximum loss = Total premium (₹7)
•   Profit potential = Unlimited (upside) and substantial (downside)

1. Borrow USD (lower interest rate = 3%)
2. Convert USD to INR at spot rate (₹83)
3. Invest in India at 6%
4. Enter forward contract to buy USD at ₹85
5. At maturity:
o Investment grows at higher rate
o Repay USD loan at lower rate
o   Profit arises from rate difference
b   Q.
A European Call and Put option on a non-dividend paying stock have the following
details:
•   Current Stock Price (S) = ₹120
•   Strike Price (K) = ₹110
• Time to Expiry (T) = 1 year
•   Risk-free Interest Rate (r) = 10% p.a.
• Market Price of Call Option (C) = ₹15
• Market Price of Put Option (P) = ₹5
Required:
1. State the Put–Call Parity relationship.
2. Verify whether the given option prices satisfy the parity condition.
3.   If not, identify the arbitrage opportunity.
4. Suggest the arbitrage strategy with steps. (10 Marks)
********** 9 **********
--- page 10 ---
Answer
1. Put–Call Parity Formula (2 Marks)
𝐶+ 𝐾𝑒−𝑟𝑇= 𝑃+ 𝑆
2. Verification of Parity (4 Marks)
Step 1: Calculate Present Value of Strike Price
𝐾𝑒−𝑟𝑇= 110 × 𝑒−0.10
𝑒−0.10 ≈0.9048
= 110 × 0.9048 = ₹99.53
Step 2: Compute LHS and RHS
• LHS = C + PV(K) = 15 + 99.53 = ₹114.53
• RHS = P + S = 5 + 120 = ₹125
3. Arbitrage Identification (2 Marks)
• LHS < RHS → ₹114.53 < ₹125
Arbitrage Opportunity Exists
4. Arbitrage Strategy (2 Marks)
Steps:
1. Buy Call + Invest PV(K)
2. Sell Put + Sell Stock
At maturity:
•   Positions offset, and profit arises due to price mismatch
Q7
a   Q.
An Indian importer enters into a forward contract with a bank to purchase USD
1,00,000 at a forward rate of ₹84 per USD for delivery after 3 months.
However, due to urgent payment requirements, the importer requests early
delivery after 2 months. At that time, the following rates prevail:
•   Spot Rate = ₹83.50 per USD
• 1-month forward premium = ₹0.20
Required:
1. Explain the concept of an early delivery (forward cancellation and
rebooking) in forex.
2. Calculate the rate at which early delivery will be executed.
3. Determine the gain or loss to the importer due to early delivery.
4. Briefly explain why banks charge or adjust rates for early delivery. (10
Marks)
Answer
1. Concept of Early Delivery (2 Marks)
•   Early delivery occurs when a forward contract is settled before its original
maturity.
•   It involves:
1. Cancellation of existing forward contract
2. Rebooking at current applicable rates
2. Calculation of Early Delivery Rate (4 Marks)
Step 1: Adjust Spot Rate for Forward Premium
Since 1 month is remaining:
𝐴𝑑𝑗𝑢𝑠𝑡𝑒𝑑 𝑅𝑎𝑡𝑒= 𝑆𝑝𝑜𝑡+ 𝑃𝑟𝑒𝑚𝑖𝑢𝑚= 83.50 + 0.20 = ₹83.70
********** 10 **********
--- page 11 ---
Early Delivery Rate = ₹83.70 per USD
3. Gain/Loss Calculation (2 Marks)
•   Original Contract Rate = ₹84
•   Early Delivery Rate = ₹83.70
𝑇𝑜𝑡𝑎𝑙 𝐺𝑎𝑖𝑛= 0.30 × 1,00,000 = ₹30,000
Importer gains ₹30,000
4. Reason for Adjustment (2 Marks)
• Banks adjust rates due to:
1. Interest rate differentials (cost of carry)
2. Market movement in spot and forward rates
3. Compensation for breaking original contract terms

A European Call option on a non-dividend paying stock has the following details:

Q1   Answer briefly.   Marks
CO-1, SO-1, a   “A strong regulatory framework in the underlying market leads to stable and 05
BL-   reliable derivatives trading.” Discuss the influence of regulation.
Understand
Answer: (Reward 1 mark for every correct point)
•   Regulation ensures fair trading practices and transparency.
• Reduces fraud, manipulation, and insider trading.
• Enhances investor confidence and participation.
•   Provides risk management tools like margining systems.
• Example: SEBI regulations in India ensure orderly derivatives markets.
•   Conclusion: Strong regulation of underlying markets supports stable
derivatives trading.
CO-1, SO-1, b A bank lends to two firms:   05
BL-
Understand   Firm A: High profits but volatile cash flows
& Apply   Firm B: Moderate profits but stable cash flows
Which firm has higher credit risk? Explain conceptually.
Answer:
Credit risk depends more on cash flow stability than profitability (1 mark)
Firm A: High profits but uncertain repayment ability leads to higher probability of
default (1.5 mark)
Firm B: Predictable cash flows expected better debt servicing capacity (1.5 mark)
Firm A has higher credit risk due to volatility. (1 mark)
********** 1 **********
--- page 2 ---
CO-1, SO-1,   c   A call option has a strike price of ₹200. The current market price is ₹230.   05
BL-Analyse   Explain the Call option from buyers and sellers prospective.
Determine moneyness and calculate intrinsic value.
Answer:
A call option gives the buyer (holder) the right, but not the obligation, to buy an
underlying asset at a predetermined strike price on or before expiry. (1 mark)
Buyer of Call Option (Holder) (1.5 marks)
Position: Pays premium to acquire the right to buy
View: Bullish (expects price to rise)
Payoff:
Profit when Spot Price > Strike Price
Loss limited to premium paid
Seller of Call Option (Writer) (1.5 marks)
Position: Receives premium and has obligation to sell
View: Bearish/Neutral (expects price to stay same or fall)
Payoff:
Profit limited to premium received
Loss occurs when Spot Price > Strike Price
Since Spot (230) > Strike (200) = ITM Call Option (1 mark)
Intrinsic Value = Spot – Strike = 230 – 200 = ₹30
CO-1, SO-1, d   Explain the concept of Financial Risk. State its objectives and briefly discuss the 05
BL-Analyse   major methods used to manage financial risk. (5 Marks)
1. Meaning of Financial Risk (1 Mark)
•   Financial risk refers to the possibility of loss arising due to fluctuations in
financial variables such as interest rates, exchange rates, credit defaults,
or market prices.
2. Objectives of Financial Risk Management (1 Mark)
1. To reduce financial uncertainty
2. To safeguard business value and earnings
3. To ensure stability in cash flows
3. Key Methods of Managing Financial Risk (3 Marks)
1. Hedging – Using financial instruments like futures, options, and swaps to
offset potential losses
2. Diversification – Spreading investments across different assets to reduce
exposure to any single risk
3. Risk Transfer – Passing risk to another party through insurance or
contractual agreements
4. Risk Acceptance – Bearing the risk when it is within acceptable limits and
cost of mitigation is high

a) Calculate the one-year forward exchange rate using the Fisher Effect. (5 marks)
b) Calculate the forward exchange rate for a 180-day period. (5 marks)

BL-Analyse   A fund manager holds a diversified equity portfolio worth ₹80,00,000. The
portfolio has a Beta (β) of 0.9. The current value of the stock index is 25,000, and
each index futures contract is valued at ₹2,50,000.
The fund manager anticipates a market decline and decides to hedge the portfolio
using index futures.
Required:
1. Calculate the number of futures contracts required to hedge the portfolio.
2.   If the market falls by 8%, compute:
o Loss on portfolio
o Gain on futures position
3. Comment on the effectiveness of the hedge.
Answer
1. Number of Futures Contracts (4 Marks)
Formula:
𝛽× Value of Portfolio
𝑁=
Value of One Futures Contract
0.9 × 80,00,000   72,00,000
𝑁=   =   = 28.8 ≈29 contracts
2,50,000   2,50,000
Action: Sell 29 futures contracts (to hedge against a fall).
2. Gain/Loss Calculation (3 Marks)
(a) Loss on Portfolio
Loss = 80,00,000 × 8% × 0.9 = ₹5,76,000
(b) Gain on Futures
Gain = 29 × (8% × 2,50,000) = 29 × 20,000 = ₹5,80,000
3. Effectiveness of Hedge (3 Marks)
•   Loss on portfolio = ₹5,76,000
•   Gain on futures = ₹5,80,000
Net Effect = ₹4,000 (small surplus gain)

B. A derivatives trader expects that the stock price of Infosys Ltd. will not rise
substantially in the short term. To generate income, the trader writes call

₹25 per share. Each lot consists of 100 shares, and the trader writes a total of
3 lots.
At maturity, the stock price may be ₹1400, ₹1500, ₹1550, ₹1600, or ₹1650.
You are required to compute the total net payoff for the trader at each price

Answer:
A

CO-1, SO-1, b   Question (10 Marks)   10
BL-Analyse   Explain the concepts of Expected Return and Standard Deviation. Calculate the
same for the following data and comment.
Economic Condition Probability Return (%)
Boom   0.25   10
Normal   0.50   14
Slowdown   0.15   18
********** 4 **********
--- page 5 ---
Recession   0.10   22
Answer
1. Expected Return (E(R)) – 1 Mark
•   It is the weighted average of possible returns based on probabilities.
•   It indicates the average expected performance of an investment.
Formula:
𝐸(𝑅) = ∑𝑃𝑖× 𝑅𝑖
2. Standard Deviation (σ) – 1 Mark
•   It measures the risk or variability in returns.
•   Higher standard deviation indicates greater uncertainty (risk).
Formula:
𝜎= √∑𝑃𝑖(𝑅𝑖−𝐸(𝑅))2
3. Expected Return Calculation (2 Marks)
𝐸(𝑅) = (0.25 × 10) + (0.50 × 14) + (0.15 × 18) + (0.10 × 22)
= 2.5 + 7 + 2.7 + 2.2 = 14.4%
4. Variance & Standard Deviation Calculation (3 Marks)
Condition P   R R − E(R) (R − E(R))² P × (R − E(R))²
Boom   0.25 10 -4.4   19.36   4.84
Normal   0.50 14 -0.4   0.16   0.08
Slowdown 0.15 18 3.6   12.96   1.944
Recession 0.10 22 7.6   57.76   5.776
5. Variance & Standard Deviation (2 Marks)
𝜎2 = 4.84 + 0.08 + 1.944 + 5.776 = 12.64
𝜎= √12.64 ≈3.55%
6. Interpretation (1 Mark)
• Expected Return = 14.4%
• Standard Deviation ≈ 3.55% (low to moderate risk)
Q4   20
CO-1, SO-1, a   Discuss the role of derivatives in managing interest rate risk. Explain with suitable
BL-Analyse   examples of Futures, Options, and Swaps. [10 marks]
Interest rate risk arises due to fluctuations in market interest rates, which affect
borrowing costs and investment returns. Derivatives are important tools used to
hedge this risk and ensure financial stability.
Interest Rate Futures:
These contracts help lock in interest rates and protect against adverse
movements.
Example:
A bank expects interest rates to rise and sells bond futures. When interest rates
increase, bond prices fall, resulting in gains in futures. This offsets losses in the
bond portfolio.
********** 5 **********
--- page 6 ---
Interest Rate Options
Options such as caps and floors provide flexible hedging.
Example:
A company with a floating rate loan purchases an interest rate cap.
If rates exceed a certain level, the company receives compensation.
If rates fall, the company benefits from lower payments.
Interest Rate Swaps
Swaps allow exchange of fixed and floating interest payments.
Example:
A company with a floating rate loan fears rising rates and enters into a swap to
pay fixed and receive floating. This converts uncertain payments into fixed
obligations.

BL-Analyse   price of ₹2,200 per share. Calculate the P&L if the spot price at expiry varies
from ₹1,900 to ₹2,500 in multiples of ₹100. Draw and explain the payoff
structure. [5 marks]
Answer:
P&L = Spot Price – Futures Price
Spot
Price   P&L (₹)
(₹)
1900   -300
2000   -200
2100   -100
2200   0
2300   100
2400   200
2500   300
Linear increasing payoff
Break-even will be ₹2,200
B. Mr. Pento enters into a short futures contract on Infosys Ltd at a strike price

BL-Analyse   A. Option Classification (5 Marks)
Identify whether the following options are In-The-Money (ITM), At-The-Money
(ATM), or Out-of-The-Money (OTM) for the buyer. Also state whether the option
will be exercised or lapse. Treat each case independently.
a.   TCS   3200   CALL   when   the   price   on   expiry   is   ₹3350
b.   INFY   1500   PUT   when   the   price   on   expiry   is   ₹1500
c.   HDFC   2800   CALL when   the   price   on   expiry   is   ₹2750
d.   SBI   600   PUT   when   the   price   on   expiry   is   ₹560
e. ICICI 900 CALL when the price on expiry is ₹920
Answer
A. Classification Table (5 Marks)
Sr. No. Market Price Option Type Exercise Price Decision ITM / ATM / OTM
1   3350   CALL   3200   Exercise ITM
2   1500   PUT   1500   Lapse ATM
3   2750   CALL   2800   Lapse OTM
4   560   PUT   600   Exercise ITM
5   920   CALL   900   Exercise ITM
Key Logic (for examiner clarity)
1. Call Option:
o ITM → Market Price > Strike Price → Exercise
o OTM → Market Price < Strike Price → Lapse
2. Put Option:
o ITM → Market Price < Strike Price → Exercise
o OTM → Market Price > Strike Price → Lapse
********** 9 **********
--- page 10 ---
3. ATM:
o Market Price = Strike Price → Generally Lapse
B Explain Theta. (5 marks)
1. Meaning (Definition)
• Theta measures the rate at which an option’s value declines with the
passage of time, assuming all other factors remain constant.
•   It is commonly referred to as time decay.
2. Nature of Theta
•   Theta is generally negative for option buyers (value erodes over time).
•   Theta is positive for option sellers (they benefit from time decay).
3. Interpretation
•   Theta indicates how much premium will reduce per day.
•   Example: If Theta = –3, the option price decreases by ₹3 per day, ceteris
paribus.
4. Behaviour with Time to Expiry
1. Far from Expiry:
o Time decay is slow and gradual.
2. Near Expiry:
o Time decay becomes very rapid, especially in the last few days.
5. Behaviour with Moneyness
• ATM Options: Highest Theta (maximum time value decay).
• ITM / OTM Options: Lower Theta compared to ATM.

derivatives market. The following information is available:
• Spot Index (S) = 15,000
•   Risk-free Interest Rate (r) = 5% p.a.
•   Dividend Yield (q) = 2% p.a.
• Time to Maturity (T) = 3 months (0.25 years)
Required:
1. Compute the theoretical fair value of the index futures using the cost-
of-carry model.
2.   If the actual futures price in the market is 15,300, determine whether an
arbitrage opportunity exists.
3. Suggest the appropriate arbitrage strategy and explain the steps.
Answer
1. Calculation of Futures Price (4 Marks)
Formula:
𝑭= 𝑺𝒆(𝒓−𝒒)𝑻
Substitute Values
𝑭= 𝟏𝟓𝟎𝟎𝟎× 𝒆(𝟎.𝟎𝟓−𝟎.𝟎𝟐)×𝟎.𝟐𝟓
********** 10 **********
--- page 11 ---
Simplify Exponent
= 𝟏𝟓𝟎𝟎𝟎× 𝒆𝟎.𝟎𝟑×𝟎.𝟐𝟓= 𝟏𝟓𝟎𝟎𝟎× 𝒆𝟎.𝟎𝟎𝟕𝟓
Compute Value
𝒆𝟎.𝟎𝟎𝟕𝟓≈𝟏. 𝟎𝟎𝟕𝟓
𝑭= 𝟏𝟓𝟎𝟎𝟎× 𝟏.𝟎𝟎𝟕𝟓= 𝟏𝟓,𝟏𝟏𝟐.𝟓
Theoretical Futures Price = 15,112.5
2. Arbitrage Identification (3 Marks)
• Market Futures Price = 15,300
•   Theoretical Price = 15,112.5
Observation:
•   Futures are overpriced (15,300 > 15,112.5)
Conclusion:
Arbitrage Opportunity Exists
3. Arbitrage Strategy (3 Marks)
Cash-and-Carry Arbitrage (Futures Overpriced)
Steps:
1. Buy the index (spot market) at 15,000
2. Borrow funds at risk-free rate (5%)
3. Sell futures at 15,300
4. Hold the index and earn dividend yield (2%)
On Maturity:
5. Deliver index against futures contract
6. Receive ₹15,300 from futures position
7. Repay borrowed amount with interest
Profit:
•   Profit arises due to difference between higher futures price and lower
cost-of-carry price

BL-Analyse   uncertain about the direction. The trader constructs a Long Strangle using the
following data:

3. Compute the profit or loss if the stock price at expiry is:
₹120
₹150
₹180
4. Comment on the suitability and risk of the strategy.
Answer
1. Strategy Explanation (3 Marks)
• A Long Strangle is a non-directional strategy used when large price
movement is expected.
********** 11 **********
--- page 12 ---
•   It involves:
1. Buying an OTM Call option
2. Buying an OTM Put option
• Key features:
1. Different strike prices (unlike straddle)
2. Same expiry and underlying
•   Profit arises when the price moves significantly in either direction.
2. Total Cost & Breakeven (3 Marks)
Total Premium Paid
= 𝟓+ 𝟒= ₹𝟗
Breakeven Points
• Upper BEP = Call Strike + Total Premium
= 𝟏𝟔𝟎+ 𝟗= ₹𝟏𝟔𝟗
• Lower BEP = Put Strike − Total Premium
= 𝟏𝟒𝟎−𝟗= ₹𝟏𝟑𝟏
3. Profit/Loss Calculation (2 Marks)
Price = ₹120
•   Call payoff = 0
• Put payoff = 140 − 120 = 20
Net Profit = 𝟐𝟎−𝟗= ₹𝟏𝟏 (Profit)
Price = ₹150
•   Call payoff = 0
• Put payoff = 0
Net Loss = ₹𝟗 (Loss)
Price = ₹180
•   Call payoff = 180 − 160 = 20
• Put payoff = 0
Net Profit = 𝟐𝟎−𝟗= ₹𝟏𝟏 (Profit)
4. Suitability & Risk (2 Marks)
•   Suitable when high volatility is expected but direction is uncertain.
• Maximum Loss = Total premium paid (₹9)
•   Profit Potential = Unlimited (upside) and substantial (downside)

CO-1, SO-1,   c   A call option has a strike price of ₹200. The current market price is ₹230.   05
BL-Analyse   Explain the Call option from buyers and sellers prospective.
Determine moneyness and calculate intrinsic value.
Answer:
A call option gives the buyer (holder) the right, but not the obligation, to buy an
underlying asset at a predetermined strike price on or before expiry. (1 mark)
Buyer of Call Option (Holder) (1.5 marks)
Position: Pays premium to acquire the right to buy
View: Bullish (expects price to rise)
Payoff:
Profit when Spot Price > Strike Price
Loss limited to premium paid
Seller of Call Option (Writer) (1.5 marks)
Position: Receives premium and has obligation to sell
View: Bearish/Neutral (expects price to stay same or fall)
Payoff:
Profit limited to premium received
Loss occurs when Spot Price > Strike Price
Since Spot (230) > Strike (200) = ITM Call Option (1 mark)
Intrinsic Value = Spot – Strike = 230 – 200 = ₹30
CO-1, SO-1, d   Explain the concept of Financial Risk. State its objectives and briefly discuss the 05
BL-Analyse   major methods used to manage financial risk. (5 Marks)
1. Meaning of Financial Risk (1 Mark)
•   Financial risk refers to the possibility of loss arising due to fluctuations in
financial variables such as interest rates, exchange rates, credit defaults,
or market prices.
2. Objectives of Financial Risk Management (1 Mark)
1. To reduce financial uncertainty
2. To safeguard business value and earnings
3. To ensure stability in cash flows
3. Key Methods of Managing Financial Risk (3 Marks)
1. Hedging – Using financial instruments like futures, options, and swaps to
offset potential losses
2. Diversification – Spreading investments across different assets to reduce
exposure to any single risk
3. Risk Transfer – Passing risk to another party through insurance or
contractual agreements
4. Risk Acceptance – Bearing the risk when it is within acceptable limits and
cost of mitigation is high

2,50,000   2,50,000

CO-1, SO-1, b   Question (10 Marks)   10
BL-Analyse   Explain the concepts of Expected Return and Standard Deviation. Calculate the
same for the following data and comment.
Economic Condition Probability Return (%)
Boom   0.25   10
Normal   0.50   14
Slowdown   0.15   18
********** 4 **********
--- page 5 ---
Recession   0.10   22
Answer
1. Expected Return (E(R)) – 1 Mark
•   It is the weighted average of possible returns based on probabilities.
•   It indicates the average expected performance of an investment.
Formula:
𝐸(𝑅) = ∑𝑃𝑖× 𝑅𝑖
2. Standard Deviation (σ) – 1 Mark
•   It measures the risk or variability in returns.
•   Higher standard deviation indicates greater uncertainty (risk).
Formula:
𝜎= √∑𝑃𝑖(𝑅𝑖−𝐸(𝑅))2
3. Expected Return Calculation (2 Marks)
𝐸(𝑅) = (0.25 × 10) + (0.50 × 14) + (0.15 × 18) + (0.10 × 22)
= 2.5 + 7 + 2.7 + 2.2 = 14.4%
4. Variance & Standard Deviation Calculation (3 Marks)
Condition P   R R − E(R) (R − E(R))² P × (R − E(R))²
Boom   0.25 10 -4.4   19.36   4.84
Normal   0.50 14 -0.4   0.16   0.08
Slowdown 0.15 18 3.6   12.96   1.944
Recession 0.10 22 7.6   57.76   5.776
5. Variance & Standard Deviation (2 Marks)
𝜎2 = 4.84 + 0.08 + 1.944 + 5.776 = 12.64
𝜎= √12.64 ≈3.55%
6. Interpretation (1 Mark)
• Expected Return = 14.4%
• Standard Deviation ≈ 3.55% (low to moderate risk)
Q4   20
CO-1, SO-1, a   Discuss the role of derivatives in managing interest rate risk. Explain with suitable
BL-Analyse   examples of Futures, Options, and Swaps. [10 marks]
Interest rate risk arises due to fluctuations in market interest rates, which affect
borrowing costs and investment returns. Derivatives are important tools used to
hedge this risk and ensure financial stability.
Interest Rate Futures:
These contracts help lock in interest rates and protect against adverse
movements.
Example:
A bank expects interest rates to rise and sells bond futures. When interest rates
increase, bond prices fall, resulting in gains in futures. This offsets losses in the
bond portfolio.
********** 5 **********
--- page 6 ---
Interest Rate Options
Options such as caps and floors provide flexible hedging.
Example:
A company with a floating rate loan purchases an interest rate cap.
If rates exceed a certain level, the company receives compensation.
If rates fall, the company benefits from lower payments.
Interest Rate Swaps
Swaps allow exchange of fixed and floating interest payments.
Example:
A company with a floating rate loan fears rising rates and enters into a swap to
pay fixed and receive floating. This converts uncertain payments into fixed
obligations.

BL-Analyse   A. Option Classification (5 Marks)
Identify whether the following options are In-The-Money (ITM), At-The-Money
(ATM), or Out-of-The-Money (OTM) for the buyer. Also state whether the option
will be exercised or lapse. Treat each case independently.
a.   TCS   3200   CALL   when   the   price   on   expiry   is   ₹3350
b.   INFY   1500   PUT   when   the   price   on   expiry   is   ₹1500
c.   HDFC   2800   CALL when   the   price   on   expiry   is   ₹2750
d.   SBI   600   PUT   when   the   price   on   expiry   is   ₹560
e. ICICI 900 CALL when the price on expiry is ₹920
Answer
A. Classification Table (5 Marks)
Sr. No. Market Price Option Type Exercise Price Decision ITM / ATM / OTM
1   3350   CALL   3200   Exercise ITM
2   1500   PUT   1500   Lapse ATM
3   2750   CALL   2800   Lapse OTM
4   560   PUT   600   Exercise ITM
5   920   CALL   900   Exercise ITM
Key Logic (for examiner clarity)
1. Call Option:
o ITM → Market Price > Strike Price → Exercise
o OTM → Market Price < Strike Price → Lapse
2. Put Option:
o ITM → Market Price < Strike Price → Exercise
o OTM → Market Price > Strike Price → Lapse
********** 9 **********
--- page 10 ---
3. ATM:
o Market Price = Strike Price → Generally Lapse
B Explain Theta. (5 marks)
1. Meaning (Definition)
• Theta measures the rate at which an option’s value declines with the
passage of time, assuming all other factors remain constant.
•   It is commonly referred to as time decay.
2. Nature of Theta
•   Theta is generally negative for option buyers (value erodes over time).
•   Theta is positive for option sellers (they benefit from time decay).
3. Interpretation
•   Theta indicates how much premium will reduce per day.
•   Example: If Theta = –3, the option price decreases by ₹3 per day, ceteris
paribus.
4. Behaviour with Time to Expiry
1. Far from Expiry:
o Time decay is slow and gradual.
2. Near Expiry:
o Time decay becomes very rapid, especially in the last few days.
5. Behaviour with Moneyness
• ATM Options: Highest Theta (maximum time value decay).
• ITM / OTM Options: Lower Theta compared to ATM.

BL-Analyse   An institutional investor is analysing arbitrage opportunities in the stock index
derivatives market. The following information is available:
• Spot Index (S) = 15,000
•   Risk-free Interest Rate (r) = 5% p.a.
•   Dividend Yield (q) = 2% p.a.
• Time to Maturity (T) = 3 months (0.25 years)
Required:
1. Compute the theoretical fair value of the index futures using the cost-
of-carry model.
2.   If the actual futures price in the market is 15,300, determine whether an
arbitrage opportunity exists.
3. Suggest the appropriate arbitrage strategy and explain the steps.
Answer
1. Calculation of Futures Price (4 Marks)
Formula:
𝑭= 𝑺𝒆(𝒓−𝒒)𝑻
Substitute Values
𝑭= 𝟏𝟓𝟎𝟎𝟎× 𝒆(𝟎.𝟎𝟓−𝟎.𝟎𝟐)×𝟎.𝟐𝟓
********** 10 **********
--- page 11 ---
Simplify Exponent
= 𝟏𝟓𝟎𝟎𝟎× 𝒆𝟎.𝟎𝟑×𝟎.𝟐𝟓= 𝟏𝟓𝟎𝟎𝟎× 𝒆𝟎.𝟎𝟎𝟕𝟓
Compute Value
𝒆𝟎.𝟎𝟎𝟕𝟓≈𝟏. 𝟎𝟎𝟕𝟓
𝑭= 𝟏𝟓𝟎𝟎𝟎× 𝟏.𝟎𝟎𝟕𝟓= 𝟏𝟓,𝟏𝟏𝟐.𝟓
Theoretical Futures Price = 15,112.5
2. Arbitrage Identification (3 Marks)
• Market Futures Price = 15,300
•   Theoretical Price = 15,112.5
Observation:
•   Futures are overpriced (15,300 > 15,112.5)
Conclusion:
Arbitrage Opportunity Exists
3. Arbitrage Strategy (3 Marks)
Cash-and-Carry Arbitrage (Futures Overpriced)
Steps:
1. Buy the index (spot market) at 15,000
2. Borrow funds at risk-free rate (5%)
3. Sell futures at 15,300
4. Hold the index and earn dividend yield (2%)
On Maturity:
5. Deliver index against futures contract
6. Receive ₹15,300 from futures position
7. Repay borrowed amount with interest
Profit:
•   Profit arises due to difference between higher futures price and lower
cost-of-carry price

BL-Analyse   uncertain about the direction. The trader constructs a Long Strangle using the
following data:

₹120
₹150
₹180

Answer
1. Strategy Explanation (3 Marks)
• A Long Strangle is a non-directional strategy used when large price
movement is expected.
********** 11 **********
--- page 12 ---
•   It involves:
1. Buying an OTM Call option
2. Buying an OTM Put option
• Key features:
1. Different strike prices (unlike straddle)
2. Same expiry and underlying
•   Profit arises when the price moves significantly in either direction.
2. Total Cost & Breakeven (3 Marks)
Total Premium Paid
= 𝟓+ 𝟒= ₹𝟗
Breakeven Points
• Upper BEP = Call Strike + Total Premium
= 𝟏𝟔𝟎+ 𝟗= ₹𝟏𝟔𝟗
• Lower BEP = Put Strike − Total Premium
= 𝟏𝟒𝟎−𝟗= ₹𝟏𝟑𝟏
3. Profit/Loss Calculation (2 Marks)
Price = ₹120
•   Call payoff = 0
• Put payoff = 140 − 120 = 20
Net Profit = 𝟐𝟎−𝟗= ₹𝟏𝟏 (Profit)
Price = ₹150
•   Call payoff = 0
• Put payoff = 0
Net Loss = ₹𝟗 (Loss)
Price = ₹180
•   Call payoff = 180 − 160 = 20
• Put payoff = 0
Net Profit = 𝟐𝟎−𝟗= ₹𝟏𝟏 (Profit)
4. Suitability & Risk (2 Marks)
•   Suitable when high volatility is expected but direction is uncertain.
• Maximum Loss = Total premium paid (₹9)
•   Profit Potential = Unlimited (upside) and substantial (downside)

CO-1, SO-1,   c   A call option has a strike price of ₹200. The current market price is ₹230.   05
BL-Analyse   Explain the Call option from buyers and sellers prospective.
Determine moneyness and calculate intrinsic value.
Answer:
A call option gives the buyer (holder) the right, but not the obligation, to buy an
underlying asset at a predetermined strike price on or before expiry. (1 mark)
Buyer of Call Option (Holder) (1.5 marks)
Position: Pays premium to acquire the right to buy
View: Bullish (expects price to rise)
Payoff:
Profit when Spot Price > Strike Price
Loss limited to premium paid
Seller of Call Option (Writer) (1.5 marks)
Position: Receives premium and has obligation to sell
View: Bearish/Neutral (expects price to stay same or fall)
Payoff:
Profit limited to premium received
Loss occurs when Spot Price > Strike Price
Since Spot (230) > Strike (200) = ITM Call Option (1 mark)
Intrinsic Value = Spot – Strike = 230 – 200 = ₹30
CO-1, SO-1, d   Explain the concept of Financial Risk. State its objectives and briefly discuss the 05
BL-Analyse   major methods used to manage financial risk. (5 Marks)
1. Meaning of Financial Risk (1 Mark)
•   Financial risk refers to the possibility of loss arising due to fluctuations in
financial variables such as interest rates, exchange rates, credit defaults,
or market prices.
2. Objectives of Financial Risk Management (1 Mark)
1. To reduce financial uncertainty
2. To safeguard business value and earnings
3. To ensure stability in cash flows
3. Key Methods of Managing Financial Risk (3 Marks)
1. Hedging – Using financial instruments like futures, options, and swaps to
offset potential losses
2. Diversification – Spreading investments across different assets to reduce
exposure to any single risk
3. Risk Transfer – Passing risk to another party through insurance or
contractual agreements
4. Risk Acceptance – Bearing the risk when it is within acceptable limits and
cost of mitigation is high

Answer
1. Expected Return (E(R)) – 1 Mark
•   It is the weighted average of possible returns based on probabilities.
•   It indicates the average expected performance of an investment.
Formula:
𝐸(𝑅) = ∑𝑃𝑖× 𝑅𝑖
2. Standard Deviation (σ) – 1 Mark
•   It measures the risk or variability in returns.
•   Higher standard deviation indicates greater uncertainty (risk).
Formula:
𝜎= √∑𝑃𝑖(𝑅𝑖−𝐸(𝑅))2
3. Expected Return Calculation (2 Marks)
𝐸(𝑅) = (0.25 × 10) + (0.50 × 14) + (0.15 × 18) + (0.10 × 22)
= 2.5 + 7 + 2.7 + 2.2 = 14.4%
4. Variance & Standard Deviation Calculation (3 Marks)
Condition P   R R − E(R) (R − E(R))² P × (R − E(R))²
Boom   0.25 10 -4.4   19.36   4.84
Normal   0.50 14 -0.4   0.16   0.08
Slowdown 0.15 18 3.6   12.96   1.944
Recession 0.10 22 7.6   57.76   5.776
5. Variance & Standard Deviation (2 Marks)
𝜎2 = 4.84 + 0.08 + 1.944 + 5.776 = 12.64
𝜎= √12.64 ≈3.55%
6. Interpretation (1 Mark)
• Expected Return = 14.4%
• Standard Deviation ≈ 3.55% (low to moderate risk)
Q4   20
CO-1, SO-1, a   Discuss the role of derivatives in managing interest rate risk. Explain with suitable
BL-Analyse   examples of Futures, Options, and Swaps. [10 marks]
Interest rate risk arises due to fluctuations in market interest rates, which affect
borrowing costs and investment returns. Derivatives are important tools used to
hedge this risk and ensure financial stability.
Interest Rate Futures:
These contracts help lock in interest rates and protect against adverse
movements.
Example:
A bank expects interest rates to rise and sells bond futures. When interest rates
increase, bond prices fall, resulting in gains in futures. This offsets losses in the
bond portfolio.
********** 5 **********
--- page 6 ---
Interest Rate Options
Options such as caps and floors provide flexible hedging.
Example:
A company with a floating rate loan purchases an interest rate cap.
If rates exceed a certain level, the company receives compensation.
If rates fall, the company benefits from lower payments.
Interest Rate Swaps
Swaps allow exchange of fixed and floating interest payments.
Example:
A company with a floating rate loan fears rising rates and enters into a swap to
pay fixed and receive floating. This converts uncertain payments into fixed
obligations.

CO-1, SO-1, b   Question (10 Marks)

Determine whether a spot arbitrage opportunity exists from the following
exchange rates. Assume the dollar ($) is the home currency. If yes, show the
arbitrage process and profit.
•   $/Euro = $1.20
• $/Yen = $0.008
• Yen/Euro = ¥155
Forward Exchange Rate (5 Marks)
Given:
• US 6-month risk-free rate = 6% p.a.
• Euro 6-month risk-free rate = 4% p.a.
•   Spot rate = 1 Euro = $1.20
Calculate the 6-month forward exchange rate using Interest Rate Parity.
Answer
Spot Arbitrage (5 Marks)
Cross Rate Calculation (1 Mark)
\text{Cross Rate } ($/Euro) = (Yen/Euro) \times ($/Yen) = 155 \times 0.008 = 1.24
\, ($/Euro)
• Market rate = $1.20/Euro
•   Cross rate = $1.24/Euro
Since cross rate > actual rate → Arbitrage Opportunity Exists

1/1.20 = 0.8333 𝐸𝑢𝑟𝑜
2. Convert Euro to Yen:
0.8333 × 155 = 129.17 𝑌𝑒𝑛
3. Convert Yen to Dollar:
129.17 × 0.008 = 1.0334 𝐷𝑜𝑙𝑙𝑎𝑟
Profit (1 Mark)
•   Initial = $1

Q6   20
CO-1, SO-1, a   Question (10 Marks)
BL-Analyse   A. Option Classification (5 Marks)
Identify whether the following options are In-The-Money (ITM), At-The-Money
(ATM), or Out-of-The-Money (OTM) for the buyer. Also state whether the option
will be exercised or lapse. Treat each case independently.
a.   TCS   3200   CALL   when   the   price   on   expiry   is   ₹3350
b.   INFY   1500   PUT   when   the   price   on   expiry   is   ₹1500
c.   HDFC   2800   CALL when   the   price   on   expiry   is   ₹2750
d.   SBI   600   PUT   when   the   price   on   expiry   is   ₹560
e. ICICI 900 CALL when the price on expiry is ₹920
Answer
A. Classification Table (5 Marks)
Sr. No. Market Price Option Type Exercise Price Decision ITM / ATM / OTM
1   3350   CALL   3200   Exercise ITM
2   1500   PUT   1500   Lapse ATM
3   2750   CALL   2800   Lapse OTM
4   560   PUT   600   Exercise ITM
5   920   CALL   900   Exercise ITM
Key Logic (for examiner clarity)
1. Call Option:
o ITM → Market Price > Strike Price → Exercise
o OTM → Market Price < Strike Price → Lapse
2. Put Option:
o ITM → Market Price < Strike Price → Exercise
o OTM → Market Price > Strike Price → Lapse
********** 9 **********
--- page 10 ---
3. ATM:
o Market Price = Strike Price → Generally Lapse
B Explain Theta. (5 marks)
1. Meaning (Definition)
• Theta measures the rate at which an option’s value declines with the
passage of time, assuming all other factors remain constant.
•   It is commonly referred to as time decay.
2. Nature of Theta
•   Theta is generally negative for option buyers (value erodes over time).
•   Theta is positive for option sellers (they benefit from time decay).
3. Interpretation
•   Theta indicates how much premium will reduce per day.
•   Example: If Theta = –3, the option price decreases by ₹3 per day, ceteris
paribus.
4. Behaviour with Time to Expiry
1. Far from Expiry:
o Time decay is slow and gradual.
2. Near Expiry:
o Time decay becomes very rapid, especially in the last few days.
5. Behaviour with Moneyness
• ATM Options: Highest Theta (maximum time value decay).
• ITM / OTM Options: Lower Theta compared to ATM.
CO-1, SO-1, b   Question (10 Marks)
BL-Analyse   An institutional investor is analysing arbitrage opportunities in the stock index
derivatives market. The following information is available:
• Spot Index (S) = 15,000
•   Risk-free Interest Rate (r) = 5% p.a.
•   Dividend Yield (q) = 2% p.a.
• Time to Maturity (T) = 3 months (0.25 years)
Required:
1. Compute the theoretical fair value of the index futures using the cost-
of-carry model.
2.   If the actual futures price in the market is 15,300, determine whether an
arbitrage opportunity exists.
3. Suggest the appropriate arbitrage strategy and explain the steps.
Answer
1. Calculation of Futures Price (4 Marks)
Formula:
𝑭= 𝑺𝒆(𝒓−𝒒)𝑻
Substitute Values
𝑭= 𝟏𝟓𝟎𝟎𝟎× 𝒆(𝟎.𝟎𝟓−𝟎.𝟎𝟐)×𝟎.𝟐𝟓
********** 10 **********
--- page 11 ---
Simplify Exponent
= 𝟏𝟓𝟎𝟎𝟎× 𝒆𝟎.𝟎𝟑×𝟎.𝟐𝟓= 𝟏𝟓𝟎𝟎𝟎× 𝒆𝟎.𝟎𝟎𝟕𝟓
Compute Value
𝒆𝟎.𝟎𝟎𝟕𝟓≈𝟏. 𝟎𝟎𝟕𝟓
𝑭= 𝟏𝟓𝟎𝟎𝟎× 𝟏.𝟎𝟎𝟕𝟓= 𝟏𝟓,𝟏𝟏𝟐.𝟓
Theoretical Futures Price = 15,112.5
2. Arbitrage Identification (3 Marks)
• Market Futures Price = 15,300
•   Theoretical Price = 15,112.5
Observation:
•   Futures are overpriced (15,300 > 15,112.5)
Conclusion:
Arbitrage Opportunity Exists
3. Arbitrage Strategy (3 Marks)
Cash-and-Carry Arbitrage (Futures Overpriced)
Steps:
1. Buy the index (spot market) at 15,000
2. Borrow funds at risk-free rate (5%)
3. Sell futures at 15,300
4. Hold the index and earn dividend yield (2%)
On Maturity:
5. Deliver index against futures contract
6. Receive ₹15,300 from futures position
7. Repay borrowed amount with interest
Profit:
•   Profit arises due to difference between higher futures price and lower
cost-of-carry price
Q 7   20
CO-1, SO-1, a A trader expects significant volatility in a stock currently trading at ₹150 but is
BL-Analyse   uncertain about the direction. The trader constructs a Long Strangle using the
following data:
•   Call Option Strike Price = ₹160, Premium = ₹5
• Put Option Strike Price = ₹140, Premium = ₹4
Required:
1. Explain the Long Strangle strategy and its structure.
2. Calculate the total cost and breakeven points.
3. Compute the profit or loss if the stock price at expiry is:
₹120
₹150
₹180
4. Comment on the suitability and risk of the strategy.
Answer
1. Strategy Explanation (3 Marks)
• A Long Strangle is a non-directional strategy used when large price
movement is expected.
********** 11 **********
--- page 12 ---
•   It involves:
1. Buying an OTM Call option
2. Buying an OTM Put option
• Key features:
1. Different strike prices (unlike straddle)
2. Same expiry and underlying
•   Profit arises when the price moves significantly in either direction.
2. Total Cost & Breakeven (3 Marks)
Total Premium Paid
= 𝟓+ 𝟒= ₹𝟗
Breakeven Points
• Upper BEP = Call Strike + Total Premium
= 𝟏𝟔𝟎+ 𝟗= ₹𝟏𝟔𝟗
• Lower BEP = Put Strike − Total Premium
= 𝟏𝟒𝟎−𝟗= ₹𝟏𝟑𝟏
3. Profit/Loss Calculation (2 Marks)
Price = ₹120
•   Call payoff = 0
• Put payoff = 140 − 120 = 20
Net Profit = 𝟐𝟎−𝟗= ₹𝟏𝟏 (Profit)
Price = ₹150
•   Call payoff = 0
• Put payoff = 0
Net Loss = ₹𝟗 (Loss)
Price = ₹180
•   Call payoff = 180 − 160 = 20
• Put payoff = 0
Net Profit = 𝟐𝟎−𝟗= ₹𝟏𝟏 (Profit)
4. Suitability & Risk (2 Marks)
•   Suitable when high volatility is expected but direction is uncertain.
• Maximum Loss = Total premium paid (₹9)
•   Profit Potential = Unlimited (upside) and substantial (downside)
CO-1, SO-1, b   Question (10 Marks)
BL-Analyse   A European Call option on a non-dividend paying stock has the following details:
•   Current Stock Price (S) = ₹120
•   Exercise Price (K) = ₹110
•   Risk-free Interest Rate (r) = 6% p.a.
• Time to Expiry (T) = 1 year
•   Volatility (σ) = 25% p.a.
Additional Data:
• 𝑵(𝒅𝟏) = 𝟎.𝟕𝟓𝟗𝟓
• 𝑵(𝒅𝟐) = 𝟎.𝟔𝟔𝟔𝟒
********** 12 **********
--- page 13 ---
Required:
1. State the Black–Scholes formula for pricing a European Call option.
2. Compute the values of 𝒅𝟏and 𝒅𝟐.
3. Calculate the price of the Call option.
4. Interpret the meaning of 𝑵(𝒅𝟏)and 𝑵(𝒅𝟐).
Answer
1. Black–Scholes Formula (2 Marks)
𝑪= 𝑺𝑵(𝒅𝟏) −𝑲𝒆−𝒓𝑻𝑵(𝒅𝟐)
Where:
𝟏
𝐥𝐧⁡(𝑺/𝑲) + (𝒓+ 𝟐𝝈𝟐)𝑻
𝒅𝟏=
𝝈√𝑻
𝒅𝟐= 𝒅𝟏−𝝈√𝑻
2. Calculation of 𝒅𝟏and 𝒅𝟐(3 Marks)
Compute 𝒅𝟏
𝐥𝐧⁡(𝟏𝟐𝟎/𝟏𝟏𝟎) + (𝟎.𝟎𝟔+ 𝟎.𝟓× 𝟎.𝟐𝟓𝟐) × 𝟏
𝒅𝟏=
𝟎. 𝟐𝟓
𝐥𝐧⁡(𝟏. 𝟎𝟗𝟎𝟗) + (𝟎. 𝟎𝟔+ 𝟎.𝟎𝟑𝟏𝟐𝟓)
=
𝟎. 𝟐𝟓
𝟎. 𝟎𝟖𝟕𝟎+ 𝟎.𝟎𝟗𝟏𝟐𝟓 𝟎.𝟏𝟕𝟖𝟐𝟓
=   =   = 𝟎. 𝟕𝟏𝟑
𝟎. 𝟐𝟓   𝟎. 𝟐𝟓
Compute 𝒅𝟐
𝒅𝟐= 𝟎. 𝟕𝟏𝟑−𝟎.𝟐𝟓= 𝟎.𝟒𝟔𝟑
3. Call Option Price (3 Marks)
𝑪= 𝟏𝟐𝟎× 𝟎.𝟕𝟓𝟗𝟓−𝟏𝟏𝟎× 𝒆−𝟎.𝟎𝟔× 𝟎.𝟔𝟔𝟔𝟒
𝒆−𝟎.𝟎𝟔≈𝟎.𝟗𝟒𝟏𝟖
= 𝟗𝟏.𝟏𝟒−(𝟏𝟏𝟎× 𝟎. 𝟗𝟒𝟏𝟖× 𝟎.𝟔𝟔𝟔𝟒)
= 𝟗𝟏.𝟏𝟒−𝟔𝟖.𝟗𝟕= ₹𝟐𝟐. 𝟏𝟕
Call Option Value ≈ ₹22.17
4. Interpretation of 𝑵(𝒅𝟏)and 𝑵(𝒅𝟐)(2 Marks)
• 𝑵(𝒅𝟏):
Represents the Delta (hedge ratio)
Measures sensitivity of option price to stock price
• 𝑵(𝒅𝟐):
Represents the risk-neutral probability that the option will be
exercised

CO-1, SO-1, a   “A strong regulatory framework in the underlying market leads to stable and 05
BL-   reliable derivatives trading.” Discuss the influence of regulation.
Understand
Answer: (Reward 1 mark for every correct point)
•   Regulation ensures fair trading practices and transparency.
• Reduces fraud, manipulation, and insider trading.
• Enhances investor confidence and participation.
•   Provides risk management tools like margining systems.
• Example: SEBI regulations in India ensure orderly derivatives markets.
•   Conclusion: Strong regulation of underlying markets supports stable
derivatives trading.

& Apply   Firm B: Moderate profits but stable cash flows
Which firm has higher credit risk? Explain conceptually.
Answer:
Credit risk depends more on cash flow stability than profitability (1 mark)
Firm A: High profits but uncertain repayment ability leads to higher probability of
default (1.5 mark)
Firm B: Predictable cash flows expected better debt servicing capacity (1.5 mark)
Firm A has higher credit risk due to volatility. (1 mark)

CO-1, SO-1,   c   A call option has a strike price of ₹200. The current market price is ₹230.   05
BL-Analyse   Explain the Call option from buyers and sellers prospective.
Determine moneyness and calculate intrinsic value.
Answer:
A call option gives the buyer (holder) the right, but not the obligation, to buy an
underlying asset at a predetermined strike price on or before expiry. (1 mark)
Buyer of Call Option (Holder) (1.5 marks)
Position: Pays premium to acquire the right to buy
View: Bullish (expects price to rise)
Payoff:
Profit when Spot Price > Strike Price
Loss limited to premium paid
Seller of Call Option (Writer) (1.5 marks)
Position: Receives premium and has obligation to sell
View: Bearish/Neutral (expects price to stay same or fall)
Payoff:
Profit limited to premium received
Loss occurs when Spot Price > Strike Price
Since Spot (230) > Strike (200) = ITM Call Option (1 mark)
Intrinsic Value = Spot – Strike = 230 – 200 = ₹30
CO-1, SO-1, d   Explain the concept of Financial Risk. State its objectives and briefly discuss the 05
BL-Analyse   major methods used to manage financial risk. (5 Marks)
1. Meaning of Financial Risk (1 Mark)
•   Financial risk refers to the possibility of loss arising due to fluctuations in
financial variables such as interest rates, exchange rates, credit defaults,
or market prices.
2. Objectives of Financial Risk Management (1 Mark)
1. To reduce financial uncertainty
2. To safeguard business value and earnings
3. To ensure stability in cash flows
3. Key Methods of Managing Financial Risk (3 Marks)
1. Hedging – Using financial instruments like futures, options, and swaps to
offset potential losses
2. Diversification – Spreading investments across different assets to reduce
exposure to any single risk
3. Risk Transfer – Passing risk to another party through insurance or
contractual agreements
4. Risk Acceptance – Bearing the risk when it is within acceptable limits and
cost of mitigation is high

CO-1, SO-1, b   Question (10 Marks)
BL-Analyse   A fund manager holds a diversified equity portfolio worth ₹80,00,000. The
portfolio has a Beta (β) of 0.9. The current value of the stock index is 25,000, and
each index futures contract is valued at ₹2,50,000.
The fund manager anticipates a market decline and decides to hedge the portfolio
using index futures.
Required:
1. Calculate the number of futures contracts required to hedge the portfolio.
2.   If the market falls by 8%, compute:
o Loss on portfolio
o Gain on futures position
3. Comment on the effectiveness of the hedge.
Answer
1. Number of Futures Contracts (4 Marks)
Formula:
𝛽× Value of Portfolio
𝑁=
Value of One Futures Contract
0.9 × 80,00,000   72,00,000
𝑁=   =   = 28.8 ≈29 contracts
2,50,000   2,50,000
Action: Sell 29 futures contracts (to hedge against a fall).
2. Gain/Loss Calculation (3 Marks)
(a) Loss on Portfolio
Loss = 80,00,000 × 8% × 0.9 = ₹5,76,000
(b) Gain on Futures
Gain = 29 × (8% × 2,50,000) = 29 × 20,000 = ₹5,80,000
3. Effectiveness of Hedge (3 Marks)
•   Loss on portfolio = ₹5,76,000
•   Gain on futures = ₹5,80,000
Net Effect = ₹4,000 (small surplus gain)
