CO-1, SO-1, b   Question (10 Marks)   10
BL-Analyse   Explain the concepts of Expected Return and Standard Deviation. Calculate the
same for the following data and comment.
Economic Condition Probability Return (%)
Boom   0.25   10
Normal   0.50   14
Slowdown   0.15   18
********** 4 **********
--- page 5 ---
Recession   0.10   22
Answer
1. Expected Return (E(R)) – 1 Mark
•   It is the weighted average of possible returns based on probabilities.
•   It indicates the average expected performance of an investment.
Formula:
𝐸(𝑅) = ∑𝑃𝑖× 𝑅𝑖
2. Standard Deviation (σ) – 1 Mark
•   It measures the risk or variability in returns.
•   Higher standard deviation indicates greater uncertainty (risk).
Formula:
𝜎= √∑𝑃𝑖(𝑅𝑖−𝐸(𝑅))2
3. Expected Return Calculation (2 Marks)
𝐸(𝑅) = (0.25 × 10) + (0.50 × 14) + (0.15 × 18) + (0.10 × 22)
= 2.5 + 7 + 2.7 + 2.2 = 14.4%
4. Variance & Standard Deviation Calculation (3 Marks)
Condition P   R R − E(R) (R − E(R))² P × (R − E(R))²
Boom   0.25 10 -4.4   19.36   4.84
Normal   0.50 14 -0.4   0.16   0.08
Slowdown 0.15 18 3.6   12.96   1.944
Recession 0.10 22 7.6   57.76   5.776
5. Variance & Standard Deviation (2 Marks)
𝜎2 = 4.84 + 0.08 + 1.944 + 5.776 = 12.64
𝜎= √12.64 ≈3.55%
6. Interpretation (1 Mark)
• Expected Return = 14.4%
• Standard Deviation ≈ 3.55% (low to moderate risk)
Q4   20
CO-1, SO-1, a   Discuss the role of derivatives in managing interest rate risk. Explain with suitable
BL-Analyse   examples of Futures, Options, and Swaps. [10 marks]
Interest rate risk arises due to fluctuations in market interest rates, which affect
borrowing costs and investment returns. Derivatives are important tools used to
hedge this risk and ensure financial stability.
Interest Rate Futures:
These contracts help lock in interest rates and protect against adverse
movements.
Example:
A bank expects interest rates to rise and sells bond futures. When interest rates
increase, bond prices fall, resulting in gains in futures. This offsets losses in the
bond portfolio.
********** 5 **********
--- page 6 ---
Interest Rate Options
Options such as caps and floors provide flexible hedging.
Example:
A company with a floating rate loan purchases an interest rate cap.
If rates exceed a certain level, the company receives compensation.
If rates fall, the company benefits from lower payments.
Interest Rate Swaps
Swaps allow exchange of fixed and floating interest payments.
Example:
A company with a floating rate loan fears rising rates and enters into a swap to
pay fixed and receive floating. This converts uncertain payments into fixed
obligations.

Q6   20
CO-1, SO-1, a   Question (10 Marks)
BL-Analyse   A. Option Classification (5 Marks)
Identify whether the following options are In-The-Money (ITM), At-The-Money
(ATM), or Out-of-The-Money (OTM) for the buyer. Also state whether the option
will be exercised or lapse. Treat each case independently.
a.   TCS   3200   CALL   when   the   price   on   expiry   is   ₹3350
b.   INFY   1500   PUT   when   the   price   on   expiry   is   ₹1500
c.   HDFC   2800   CALL when   the   price   on   expiry   is   ₹2750
d.   SBI   600   PUT   when   the   price   on   expiry   is   ₹560
e. ICICI 900 CALL when the price on expiry is ₹920
Answer
A. Classification Table (5 Marks)
Sr. No. Market Price Option Type Exercise Price Decision ITM / ATM / OTM
1   3350   CALL   3200   Exercise ITM
2   1500   PUT   1500   Lapse ATM
3   2750   CALL   2800   Lapse OTM
4   560   PUT   600   Exercise ITM
5   920   CALL   900   Exercise ITM
Key Logic (for examiner clarity)
1. Call Option:
o ITM → Market Price > Strike Price → Exercise
o OTM → Market Price < Strike Price → Lapse
2. Put Option:
o ITM → Market Price < Strike Price → Exercise
o OTM → Market Price > Strike Price → Lapse
********** 9 **********
--- page 10 ---
3. ATM:
o Market Price = Strike Price → Generally Lapse
B Explain Theta. (5 marks)
1. Meaning (Definition)
• Theta measures the rate at which an option’s value declines with the
passage of time, assuming all other factors remain constant.
•   It is commonly referred to as time decay.
2. Nature of Theta
•   Theta is generally negative for option buyers (value erodes over time).
•   Theta is positive for option sellers (they benefit from time decay).
3. Interpretation
•   Theta indicates how much premium will reduce per day.
•   Example: If Theta = –3, the option price decreases by ₹3 per day, ceteris
paribus.
4. Behaviour with Time to Expiry
1. Far from Expiry:
o Time decay is slow and gradual.
2. Near Expiry:
o Time decay becomes very rapid, especially in the last few days.
5. Behaviour with Moneyness
• ATM Options: Highest Theta (maximum time value decay).
• ITM / OTM Options: Lower Theta compared to ATM.
CO-1, SO-1, b   Question (10 Marks)
BL-Analyse   An institutional investor is analysing arbitrage opportunities in the stock index
derivatives market. The following information is available:
• Spot Index (S) = 15,000
•   Risk-free Interest Rate (r) = 5% p.a.
•   Dividend Yield (q) = 2% p.a.
• Time to Maturity (T) = 3 months (0.25 years)
Required:
1. Compute the theoretical fair value of the index futures using the cost-
of-carry model.
2.   If the actual futures price in the market is 15,300, determine whether an
arbitrage opportunity exists.
3. Suggest the appropriate arbitrage strategy and explain the steps.
Answer
1. Calculation of Futures Price (4 Marks)
Formula:
𝑭= 𝑺𝒆(𝒓−𝒒)𝑻
Substitute Values
𝑭= 𝟏𝟓𝟎𝟎𝟎× 𝒆(𝟎.𝟎𝟓−𝟎.𝟎𝟐)×𝟎.𝟐𝟓
********** 10 **********
--- page 11 ---
Simplify Exponent
= 𝟏𝟓𝟎𝟎𝟎× 𝒆𝟎.𝟎𝟑×𝟎.𝟐𝟓= 𝟏𝟓𝟎𝟎𝟎× 𝒆𝟎.𝟎𝟎𝟕𝟓
Compute Value
𝒆𝟎.𝟎𝟎𝟕𝟓≈𝟏. 𝟎𝟎𝟕𝟓
𝑭= 𝟏𝟓𝟎𝟎𝟎× 𝟏.𝟎𝟎𝟕𝟓= 𝟏𝟓,𝟏𝟏𝟐.𝟓
Theoretical Futures Price = 15,112.5
2. Arbitrage Identification (3 Marks)
• Market Futures Price = 15,300
•   Theoretical Price = 15,112.5
Observation:
•   Futures are overpriced (15,300 > 15,112.5)
Conclusion:
Arbitrage Opportunity Exists
3. Arbitrage Strategy (3 Marks)
Cash-and-Carry Arbitrage (Futures Overpriced)
Steps:
1. Buy the index (spot market) at 15,000
2. Borrow funds at risk-free rate (5%)
3. Sell futures at 15,300
4. Hold the index and earn dividend yield (2%)
On Maturity:
5. Deliver index against futures contract
6. Receive ₹15,300 from futures position
7. Repay borrowed amount with interest
Profit:
•   Profit arises due to difference between higher futures price and lower
cost-of-carry price
Q 7   20
CO-1, SO-1, a A trader expects significant volatility in a stock currently trading at ₹150 but is
BL-Analyse   uncertain about the direction. The trader constructs a Long Strangle using the
following data:
•   Call Option Strike Price = ₹160, Premium = ₹5
• Put Option Strike Price = ₹140, Premium = ₹4
Required:
1. Explain the Long Strangle strategy and its structure.
2. Calculate the total cost and breakeven points.
3. Compute the profit or loss if the stock price at expiry is:
₹120
₹150
₹180
4. Comment on the suitability and risk of the strategy.
Answer
1. Strategy Explanation (3 Marks)
• A Long Strangle is a non-directional strategy used when large price
movement is expected.
********** 11 **********
--- page 12 ---
•   It involves:
1. Buying an OTM Call option
2. Buying an OTM Put option
• Key features:
1. Different strike prices (unlike straddle)
2. Same expiry and underlying
•   Profit arises when the price moves significantly in either direction.
2. Total Cost & Breakeven (3 Marks)
Total Premium Paid
= 𝟓+ 𝟒= ₹𝟗
Breakeven Points
• Upper BEP = Call Strike + Total Premium
= 𝟏𝟔𝟎+ 𝟗= ₹𝟏𝟔𝟗
• Lower BEP = Put Strike − Total Premium
= 𝟏𝟒𝟎−𝟗= ₹𝟏𝟑𝟏
3. Profit/Loss Calculation (2 Marks)
Price = ₹120
•   Call payoff = 0
• Put payoff = 140 − 120 = 20
Net Profit = 𝟐𝟎−𝟗= ₹𝟏𝟏 (Profit)
Price = ₹150
•   Call payoff = 0
• Put payoff = 0
Net Loss = ₹𝟗 (Loss)
Price = ₹180
•   Call payoff = 180 − 160 = 20
• Put payoff = 0
Net Profit = 𝟐𝟎−𝟗= ₹𝟏𝟏 (Profit)
4. Suitability & Risk (2 Marks)
•   Suitable when high volatility is expected but direction is uncertain.
• Maximum Loss = Total premium paid (₹9)
•   Profit Potential = Unlimited (upside) and substantial (downside)
CO-1, SO-1, b   Question (10 Marks)
BL-Analyse   A European Call option on a non-dividend paying stock has the following details:
•   Current Stock Price (S) = ₹120
•   Exercise Price (K) = ₹110
•   Risk-free Interest Rate (r) = 6% p.a.
• Time to Expiry (T) = 1 year
•   Volatility (σ) = 25% p.a.
Additional Data:
• 𝑵(𝒅𝟏) = 𝟎.𝟕𝟓𝟗𝟓
• 𝑵(𝒅𝟐) = 𝟎.𝟔𝟔𝟔𝟒
********** 12 **********
--- page 13 ---
Required:
1. State the Black–Scholes formula for pricing a European Call option.
2. Compute the values of 𝒅𝟏and 𝒅𝟐.
3. Calculate the price of the Call option.
4. Interpret the meaning of 𝑵(𝒅𝟏)and 𝑵(𝒅𝟐).
Answer
1. Black–Scholes Formula (2 Marks)

CO-1, SO-1,   c   A call option has a strike price of ₹200. The current market price is ₹230.   05
BL-Analyse   Explain the Call option from buyers and sellers prospective.
Determine moneyness and calculate intrinsic value.
Answer:
A call option gives the buyer (holder) the right, but not the obligation, to buy an
underlying asset at a predetermined strike price on or before expiry. (1 mark)
Buyer of Call Option (Holder) (1.5 marks)
Position: Pays premium to acquire the right to buy
View: Bullish (expects price to rise)
Payoff:
Profit when Spot Price > Strike Price
Loss limited to premium paid
Seller of Call Option (Writer) (1.5 marks)
Position: Receives premium and has obligation to sell
View: Bearish/Neutral (expects price to stay same or fall)
Payoff:
Profit limited to premium received
Loss occurs when Spot Price > Strike Price
Since Spot (230) > Strike (200) = ITM Call Option (1 mark)
Intrinsic Value = Spot – Strike = 230 – 200 = ₹30
CO-1, SO-1, d   Explain the concept of Financial Risk. State its objectives and briefly discuss the 05
BL-Analyse   major methods used to manage financial risk. (5 Marks)
1. Meaning of Financial Risk (1 Mark)
•   Financial risk refers to the possibility of loss arising due to fluctuations in
financial variables such as interest rates, exchange rates, credit defaults,
or market prices.
2. Objectives of Financial Risk Management (1 Mark)
1. To reduce financial uncertainty
2. To safeguard business value and earnings
3. To ensure stability in cash flows
3. Key Methods of Managing Financial Risk (3 Marks)
1. Hedging – Using financial instruments like futures, options, and swaps to
offset potential losses
2. Diversification – Spreading investments across different assets to reduce
exposure to any single risk
3. Risk Transfer – Passing risk to another party through insurance or
contractual agreements
4. Risk Acceptance – Bearing the risk when it is within acceptable limits and
cost of mitigation is high

portfolio has a Beta (β) of 0.9. The current value of the stock index is 25,000, and

using index futures.

o Loss on portfolio
o Gain on futures position

2,50,000   2,50,000

(a) Loss on Portfolio

CO-1, SO-1, b   Question (10 Marks)   10
BL-Analyse   Explain the concepts of Expected Return and Standard Deviation. Calculate the
same for the following data and comment.
Economic Condition Probability Return (%)
Boom   0.25   10
Normal   0.50   14
Slowdown   0.15   18
********** 4 **********
--- page 5 ---
Recession   0.10   22
Answer
1. Expected Return (E(R)) – 1 Mark
•   It is the weighted average of possible returns based on probabilities.
•   It indicates the average expected performance of an investment.
Formula:
𝐸(𝑅) = ∑𝑃𝑖× 𝑅𝑖
2. Standard Deviation (σ) – 1 Mark
•   It measures the risk or variability in returns.
•   Higher standard deviation indicates greater uncertainty (risk).
Formula:
𝜎= √∑𝑃𝑖(𝑅𝑖−𝐸(𝑅))2
3. Expected Return Calculation (2 Marks)
𝐸(𝑅) = (0.25 × 10) + (0.50 × 14) + (0.15 × 18) + (0.10 × 22)
= 2.5 + 7 + 2.7 + 2.2 = 14.4%
4. Variance & Standard Deviation Calculation (3 Marks)
Condition P   R R − E(R) (R − E(R))² P × (R − E(R))²
Boom   0.25 10 -4.4   19.36   4.84
Normal   0.50 14 -0.4   0.16   0.08
Slowdown 0.15 18 3.6   12.96   1.944
Recession 0.10 22 7.6   57.76   5.776
5. Variance & Standard Deviation (2 Marks)
𝜎2 = 4.84 + 0.08 + 1.944 + 5.776 = 12.64
𝜎= √12.64 ≈3.55%
6. Interpretation (1 Mark)
• Expected Return = 14.4%
• Standard Deviation ≈ 3.55% (low to moderate risk)
Q4   20
CO-1, SO-1, a   Discuss the role of derivatives in managing interest rate risk. Explain with suitable
BL-Analyse   examples of Futures, Options, and Swaps. [10 marks]
Interest rate risk arises due to fluctuations in market interest rates, which affect
borrowing costs and investment returns. Derivatives are important tools used to
hedge this risk and ensure financial stability.
Interest Rate Futures:
These contracts help lock in interest rates and protect against adverse
movements.
Example:
A bank expects interest rates to rise and sells bond futures. When interest rates
increase, bond prices fall, resulting in gains in futures. This offsets losses in the
bond portfolio.
********** 5 **********
--- page 6 ---
Interest Rate Options
Options such as caps and floors provide flexible hedging.
Example:
A company with a floating rate loan purchases an interest rate cap.
If rates exceed a certain level, the company receives compensation.
If rates fall, the company benefits from lower payments.
Interest Rate Swaps
Swaps allow exchange of fixed and floating interest payments.
Example:
A company with a floating rate loan fears rising rates and enters into a swap to
pay fixed and receive floating. This converts uncertain payments into fixed
obligations.

CO-1, SO-1, a   Question (10 Marks)
BL-Analyse   A. Option Classification (5 Marks)
Identify whether the following options are In-The-Money (ITM), At-The-Money
(ATM), or Out-of-The-Money (OTM) for the buyer. Also state whether the option
will be exercised or lapse. Treat each case independently.
a.   TCS   3200   CALL   when   the   price   on   expiry   is   ₹3350
b.   INFY   1500   PUT   when   the   price   on   expiry   is   ₹1500
c.   HDFC   2800   CALL when   the   price   on   expiry   is   ₹2750
d.   SBI   600   PUT   when   the   price   on   expiry   is   ₹560
e. ICICI 900 CALL when the price on expiry is ₹920
Answer
A. Classification Table (5 Marks)
Sr. No. Market Price Option Type Exercise Price Decision ITM / ATM / OTM
1   3350   CALL   3200   Exercise ITM
2   1500   PUT   1500   Lapse ATM
3   2750   CALL   2800   Lapse OTM
4   560   PUT   600   Exercise ITM
5   920   CALL   900   Exercise ITM
Key Logic (for examiner clarity)
1. Call Option:
o ITM → Market Price > Strike Price → Exercise
o OTM → Market Price < Strike Price → Lapse
2. Put Option:
o ITM → Market Price < Strike Price → Exercise
o OTM → Market Price > Strike Price → Lapse
********** 9 **********
--- page 10 ---
3. ATM:
o Market Price = Strike Price → Generally Lapse
B Explain Theta. (5 marks)
1. Meaning (Definition)
• Theta measures the rate at which an option’s value declines with the
passage of time, assuming all other factors remain constant.
•   It is commonly referred to as time decay.
2. Nature of Theta
•   Theta is generally negative for option buyers (value erodes over time).
•   Theta is positive for option sellers (they benefit from time decay).
3. Interpretation
•   Theta indicates how much premium will reduce per day.
•   Example: If Theta = –3, the option price decreases by ₹3 per day, ceteris
paribus.
4. Behaviour with Time to Expiry
1. Far from Expiry:
o Time decay is slow and gradual.
2. Near Expiry:
o Time decay becomes very rapid, especially in the last few days.
5. Behaviour with Moneyness
• ATM Options: Highest Theta (maximum time value decay).
• ITM / OTM Options: Lower Theta compared to ATM.
CO-1, SO-1, b   Question (10 Marks)
BL-Analyse   An institutional investor is analysing arbitrage opportunities in the stock index
derivatives market. The following information is available:
• Spot Index (S) = 15,000
•   Risk-free Interest Rate (r) = 5% p.a.
•   Dividend Yield (q) = 2% p.a.
• Time to Maturity (T) = 3 months (0.25 years)
Required:
1. Compute the theoretical fair value of the index futures using the cost-
of-carry model.
2.   If the actual futures price in the market is 15,300, determine whether an
arbitrage opportunity exists.
3. Suggest the appropriate arbitrage strategy and explain the steps.
Answer
1. Calculation of Futures Price (4 Marks)
Formula:
𝑭= 𝑺𝒆(𝒓−𝒒)𝑻
Substitute Values
𝑭= 𝟏𝟓𝟎𝟎𝟎× 𝒆(𝟎.𝟎𝟓−𝟎.𝟎𝟐)×𝟎.𝟐𝟓
********** 10 **********
--- page 11 ---
Simplify Exponent
= 𝟏𝟓𝟎𝟎𝟎× 𝒆𝟎.𝟎𝟑×𝟎.𝟐𝟓= 𝟏𝟓𝟎𝟎𝟎× 𝒆𝟎.𝟎𝟎𝟕𝟓
Compute Value
𝒆𝟎.𝟎𝟎𝟕𝟓≈𝟏. 𝟎𝟎𝟕𝟓
𝑭= 𝟏𝟓𝟎𝟎𝟎× 𝟏.𝟎𝟎𝟕𝟓= 𝟏𝟓,𝟏𝟏𝟐.𝟓
Theoretical Futures Price = 15,112.5
2. Arbitrage Identification (3 Marks)
• Market Futures Price = 15,300
•   Theoretical Price = 15,112.5
Observation:
•   Futures are overpriced (15,300 > 15,112.5)
Conclusion:
Arbitrage Opportunity Exists
3. Arbitrage Strategy (3 Marks)
Cash-and-Carry Arbitrage (Futures Overpriced)
Steps:
1. Buy the index (spot market) at 15,000
2. Borrow funds at risk-free rate (5%)
3. Sell futures at 15,300
4. Hold the index and earn dividend yield (2%)
On Maturity:
5. Deliver index against futures contract
6. Receive ₹15,300 from futures position
7. Repay borrowed amount with interest
Profit:
•   Profit arises due to difference between higher futures price and lower
cost-of-carry price
Q 7   20
CO-1, SO-1, a A trader expects significant volatility in a stock currently trading at ₹150 but is
BL-Analyse   uncertain about the direction. The trader constructs a Long Strangle using the
following data:
•   Call Option Strike Price = ₹160, Premium = ₹5
• Put Option Strike Price = ₹140, Premium = ₹4
Required:
1. Explain the Long Strangle strategy and its structure.
2. Calculate the total cost and breakeven points.
3. Compute the profit or loss if the stock price at expiry is:
₹120
₹150
₹180
4. Comment on the suitability and risk of the strategy.
Answer
1. Strategy Explanation (3 Marks)
• A Long Strangle is a non-directional strategy used when large price
movement is expected.
********** 11 **********
--- page 12 ---
•   It involves:
1. Buying an OTM Call option
2. Buying an OTM Put option
• Key features:
1. Different strike prices (unlike straddle)
2. Same expiry and underlying
•   Profit arises when the price moves significantly in either direction.
2. Total Cost & Breakeven (3 Marks)
Total Premium Paid
= 𝟓+ 𝟒= ₹𝟗
Breakeven Points
• Upper BEP = Call Strike + Total Premium
= 𝟏𝟔𝟎+ 𝟗= ₹𝟏𝟔𝟗
• Lower BEP = Put Strike − Total Premium
= 𝟏𝟒𝟎−𝟗= ₹𝟏𝟑𝟏
3. Profit/Loss Calculation (2 Marks)
Price = ₹120
•   Call payoff = 0
• Put payoff = 140 − 120 = 20
Net Profit = 𝟐𝟎−𝟗= ₹𝟏𝟏 (Profit)
Price = ₹150
•   Call payoff = 0
• Put payoff = 0
Net Loss = ₹𝟗 (Loss)
Price = ₹180
•   Call payoff = 180 − 160 = 20
• Put payoff = 0
Net Profit = 𝟐𝟎−𝟗= ₹𝟏𝟏 (Profit)
4. Suitability & Risk (2 Marks)
•   Suitable when high volatility is expected but direction is uncertain.
• Maximum Loss = Total premium paid (₹9)
•   Profit Potential = Unlimited (upside) and substantial (downside)
CO-1, SO-1, b   Question (10 Marks)
BL-Analyse   A European Call option on a non-dividend paying stock has the following details:
•   Current Stock Price (S) = ₹120
•   Exercise Price (K) = ₹110
•   Risk-free Interest Rate (r) = 6% p.a.
• Time to Expiry (T) = 1 year
•   Volatility (σ) = 25% p.a.
Additional Data:
• 𝑵(𝒅𝟏) = 𝟎.𝟕𝟓𝟗𝟓
• 𝑵(𝒅𝟐) = 𝟎.𝟔𝟔𝟔𝟒
********** 12 **********
--- page 13 ---
Required:
1. State the Black–Scholes formula for pricing a European Call option.
2. Compute the values of 𝒅𝟏and 𝒅𝟐.
3. Calculate the price of the Call option.
4. Interpret the meaning of 𝑵(𝒅𝟏)and 𝑵(𝒅𝟐).
Answer
1. Black–Scholes Formula (2 Marks)
𝑪= 𝑺𝑵(𝒅𝟏) −𝑲𝒆−𝒓𝑻𝑵(𝒅𝟐)
Where:
𝟏
𝐥𝐧⁡(𝑺/𝑲) + (𝒓+ 𝟐𝝈𝟐)𝑻
𝒅𝟏=
𝝈√𝑻
𝒅𝟐= 𝒅𝟏−𝝈√𝑻
2. Calculation of 𝒅𝟏and 𝒅𝟐(3 Marks)
Compute 𝒅𝟏
𝐥𝐧⁡(𝟏𝟐𝟎/𝟏𝟏𝟎) + (𝟎.𝟎𝟔+ 𝟎.𝟓× 𝟎.𝟐𝟓𝟐) × 𝟏
𝒅𝟏=
𝟎. 𝟐𝟓
𝐥𝐧⁡(𝟏. 𝟎𝟗𝟎𝟗) + (𝟎. 𝟎𝟔+ 𝟎.𝟎𝟑𝟏𝟐𝟓)
=
𝟎. 𝟐𝟓
𝟎. 𝟎𝟖𝟕𝟎+ 𝟎.𝟎𝟗𝟏𝟐𝟓 𝟎.𝟏𝟕𝟖𝟐𝟓
=   =   = 𝟎. 𝟕𝟏𝟑
𝟎. 𝟐𝟓   𝟎. 𝟐𝟓
Compute 𝒅𝟐
𝒅𝟐= 𝟎. 𝟕𝟏𝟑−𝟎.𝟐𝟓= 𝟎.𝟒𝟔𝟑
3. Call Option Price (3 Marks)
𝑪= 𝟏𝟐𝟎× 𝟎.𝟕𝟓𝟗𝟓−𝟏𝟏𝟎× 𝒆−𝟎.𝟎𝟔× 𝟎.𝟔𝟔𝟔𝟒
𝒆−𝟎.𝟎𝟔≈𝟎.𝟗𝟒𝟏𝟖
= 𝟗𝟏.𝟏𝟒−(𝟏𝟏𝟎× 𝟎. 𝟗𝟒𝟏𝟖× 𝟎.𝟔𝟔𝟔𝟒)
= 𝟗𝟏.𝟏𝟒−𝟔𝟖.𝟗𝟕= ₹𝟐𝟐. 𝟏𝟕
Call Option Value ≈ ₹22.17
4. Interpretation of 𝑵(𝒅𝟏)and 𝑵(𝒅𝟐)(2 Marks)
• 𝑵(𝒅𝟏):
Represents the Delta (hedge ratio)
Measures sensitivity of option price to stock price
• 𝑵(𝒅𝟐):
Represents the risk-neutral probability that the option will be
exercised
